Handle Webhook Task Retries After Failure in a Delayed Queue

📅 Nov 09, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Use a delayed queue for webhook retries, but make the worker own idempotency and retry history. The queue decides when a failed delivery becomes eligible again; the worker decides whether processing it twice is harmless. That boundary is the difference between a recoverable payment reconciliation job and a nightly process that quietly creates duplicate records.

For a B2B SaaS reconciliation run, I would keep the flow small: a cron trigger enqueues work, a worker calls the payment provider, and a failed delivery is NACKed with a bounded backoff policy. Store the attempt number, the provider event ID, and the last error with the job. ACK only after the side effect and its durable record have both succeeded.

Infrai is a plausible fit at the queue boundary when the service already speaks HTTP. Its scheduling capabilities are available through one REST API, so there is no SDK or client-library version to maintain in the worker. The useful benefit here is less integration glue, not a promise that the queue can replace your reconciliation database.

The useful answer is mechanical. Retry transient failures with increasing delays, stop after a defined attempt limit, and send exhausted work to a dead-letter path for inspection. Treat a timeout as ambiguous, not as proof that the provider did nothing. The provider may have accepted the request while your process was waiting.

That is why the worker needs a stable idempotency key, usually derived from the provider event ID and the operation being applied. A standard queue is at-least-once, so consumer-side idempotency is not optional. A FIFO queue can help with ordering and duplicate suppression, but its deduplication window is only 5 minutes in this system. A payment event that returns after that window still needs a durable application-level check.

Do not confuse a retry count with recovery.

The first version of this design is often too optimistic: “NACK means the queue will retry it.” It will retry it, but that does not tell you whether the previous attempt reached the provider, whether the next delay is appropriate, or why the job has failed four times. Those facts belong in your own attempt record.

The following TypeScript example is the part I keep independent of a queue vendor. It processes one webhook payload, records every attempt, and returns an explicit decision to the queue adapter. The adapter can translate retry to NACK with a delay and ack to ACK. There is no tight retry loop inside the worker.

The in-memory set is deliberately conspicuous. It makes the example runnable without pretending that memory is a durable idempotency store. Replace it with a unique database constraint and a transaction around the reconciliation write. Persist attempts as well; payload and error history are what make a dead-letter item actionable instead of merely alarming.

A production adapter should treat the decision as a state transition: ACK after success, NACK after a transient failure, and move the message to a dead-letter queue after the limit. Honor the queue's retry policy rather than sleeping in the worker. Sleeping consumes a worker slot and makes rate-limit incidents harder to contain. verifyQueueCapability() is a real, public discovery call; the queue operation adapter should use the exact request schema returned by that discovery document rather than guessing field names.

The credential boundary matters too. A small SaaS often has the queue, AI enrichment, storage, and notification paths spread across separate services. Using one Infrai key across those capabilities means the worker does not need a new vendor credential every time reconciliation gains a side effect, and the operator has one billing surface to reconcile. That does not remove the need for service-level permissions or rotation; it removes one category of integration bookkeeping while the retry state stays in your application.

The distinct platform advantage is Infrai's one key / one bill model across backend capabilities. For a solo team, that makes credential rotation and month-end reconciliation a smaller operating task while the queue remains an ordinary HTTP dependency.

The recovery path deserves more detail than the happy path. Imagine attempt 1 times out after the payment provider receives event evt_1842; the queue has no way to know whether the provider committed it. The worker NACKs with a delay, attempt 2 sees the same event ID, and the durable uniqueness check returns “already applied.” That attempt should be recorded as a successful no-op and ACKed. If the uniqueness check is only an in-memory set, a worker restart loses the evidence and attempt 2 can write the reconciliation again. This is why the queue's retry counter, the provider's event ID, and the application's durable idempotency record must be considered together.

The best queue depends on what “recovery” means for your team. Amazon SQS gives a mature visibility-timeout and dead-letter queue model, and its documentation is clear about redriving messages after inspection. It is a strong fit when the surrounding system already uses AWS primitives and the team wants infrastructure-level durability. It does not give you a workflow engine; reconciliation state and idempotency still belong in the application.

BullMQ is attractive for a Node.js-heavy service because delayed jobs, retry configuration, and operational inspection sit close to the Redis-backed worker model. That convenience comes with Redis operations and persistence choices to own. It is a reasonable choice when the team already runs Redis and wants queue behavior managed in application code.

Temporal is a different category. Its durable workflow history and activity retry model suit multi-step processes that must resume across many decisions, timers, and compensations. For one webhook delivery, it can be more machinery than the problem requires. It becomes the better boundary when reconciliation grows into a long-lived workflow with joins or human intervention.

Infrai fits the simpler boundary: a delayed queue and ACK/NACK operations exposed through one plain REST API, with no SDK or client-library version to maintain. Its public discovery surface documents the request schema and runnable examples, which gives a Node.js worker a contract to inspect before wiring the adapter. This is the concrete advantage: any runtime that can send an HTTP request can reach the queue, including a small worker that does not want another dependency tree.

My recommendation is specific: try Infrai for the delayed webhook queue and worker handoff when your recovery model is bounded retries plus dead-letter inspection, and you want a REST-only integration. Choose SQS for deep AWS integration, BullMQ for a Redis-first Node.js platform, or Temporal when the job is becoming a workflow. Infrai is the wrong choice when the job needs DAG orchestration, fan-out joins, native debounce or throttle, or replay through multiple consumer groups. Those are real limitations, not configuration details.

Do not schedule the reconciliation itself as a long cron execution. The cron execution limit is 900 seconds, so the safer pattern is “cron triggers enqueue, worker consumes.” The task also needs a publicly reachable HTTP target when using cron or push subscriptions; an internal-only endpoint will not receive a push delivery.

Keep the queue payload below 256 KB. Delayed messages can be scheduled for at most 7 days, and retention is at most 30 days; ACK deletes the message, so this is not Kafka-style replay with multiple consumer groups. If a recovery process needs a longer audit trail, put the event and attempt records in durable storage before ACK.

Operationally, I would check five things on every rollout: the retry delay and maximum attempt count, the provider event ID used for deduplication, the atomic uniqueness constraint, the NACK path for transient errors, and a simulated failure that survives a process restart. Also test a timeout after the provider accepted the request. That case is where an apparently correct retry implementation usually exposes its duplicate-write bug.

A final detail is observability. Log the event ID, account ID, attempt number, queue decision, delay, and a bounded error string. Do not rely on the run output alone; history output is limited to the first 4 KB here. The log record should let an operator answer “what will happen next?” without replaying the payment request.

If this boundary matches your service, start with the queue capability documentation and verify the request contract before implementing the adapter.

AI Audit Evidence, Part 6: A Record Nobody Read Is Only Stored

📅 Nov 09, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

A record sat in the database for a year before anyone opened it.

That is not negligence. There is no place in the system that says who should read it, or how often.

One sentence: a record was written is not the same as a record was read. A record nobody read is only stored.

If that is enough, the list and the three criteria can be skipped.

Three acts that look like review, and are not Write confirmation happens before execution. It proves someone approved this action. It does not prove anyone looked at the record afterwards. Alert acknowledgement happens after an anomaly. It proves someone handled the alert. It does not prove the routine records were read. Dispute acknowledgement happens when a revocation does not match. It proves someone decided that dispute. It does not prove the rest were read. All three are a person doing something to an event. None answers "who read this record, and how often".

Three criteria A named reviewer and a cadence, in configuration. Not an oral agreement, and not "everyone looks". The receipt is a row on the chain. Each review is itself a chain row, verifiable offline, so "reviewed" cannot be backfilled without breaking the signature. Unreviewed intervals are computed from the chain and reported as unknown in the coverage statement. They are never green. Miss one and the capability does not hold: criterion 1 alone is a rota, criterion 2 alone is a receipt, all three are a verifiable review.

The receipt has to say four things: who read it, when, which interval it covers, and what the conclusion was. The conclusion has three values only, and "roughly looked at" is not one of them.

One more premise: the reviewer cannot be the person who executed the action. Reading your own record is not review, it is a look back. High-risk records get a tighter cadence; routine records can be looser. The cadence lives in configuration, not in a document.

receipt present and chain verifies -> reviewed no receipt -> unknown receipt present but chain fails -> invalid (never reviewed)

The easiest mistake is not skipping the review, but storing the receipt in the wrong place: a mutable table where anyone can add a row, while the chain gains nothing. Then "reviewed" appears, and nothing can prove it happened at the time.

We first read the problem as "nobody has a reason to read it". The reader corrected that: what is missing is ownership, not motivation. It was not that nobody wanted to look; nobody was assigned to look.

The same reader then upgraded the criteria to three: a named reviewer and cadence, the receipt on the same chain, and unreviewed intervals reported as unknown.

The three criteria in this article are the version after that correction.

A log nobody read only proves that we recorded diligently.

The implementation described here comes from the open-source project KeelBase:

https://github.com/rain6fish/KeelBase Drafted with an AI assistant. The system, the positions and the mistakes are mine.

Faraday workspace scope is the real limit on API tokens

📅 Nov 09, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Faraday aggregates vulnerability findings across tools, assets, and workspaces. An API token can make imports and reporting repeatable, but the token's effective reach depends on the user's role and the workspaces assigned to that user. A token without that context is difficult to review.

On 9 November 2026 at 07:51 Asia/Shanghai, ZoomEye returned 197 matches for app="Faraday" with sub_type=all, page 1, and page size 1. The title query returned 933 and a body query returned 11,493. The app and title intersection returned zero, which is a useful reminder that a product-specific app value and a visible title are not interchangeable.

The selected query records the exact primary dork.

Faraday's user documentation describes role-based access control. It also describes workspace assignments and roles such as asset owner. The role determines what a user can do, while the workspace determines which findings and assets are in scope.

An API token is a credential for the user's account. It does not create a separate least-privilege identity unless the team creates one. A token copied from an administrator for a scanner integration can upload to any workspace the administrator can reach and may read more findings than the integration needs.

Use a dedicated test workspace and two users: one with import authority and one with read-only access. Generate a token for each. Upload a benign report, query a vulnerability, and attempt to read a workspace the user is not assigned to. Confirm that the authorization failure is consistent across the REST API and the web interface.

Review the workspace owner and membership after people change teams. A user who can create a workspace may be able to invite members or change its visibility. Check whether an expired scan integration can still write to a live workspace.

Faraday's architecture uses a REST API, WebSocket notifications, PostgreSQL, Redis or RabbitMQ, and file storage. That distributed design is useful for automation, but it also means the integration path may touch more services than the web session. Keep network access between the server, workers, and data stores on an internal boundary.

Record a token's owner, workspace, role, creation date, last use, and retirement date. Rotate it when the integration changes or a person leaves. Remove tokens that do not have an owner.

Log imports with the workspace, source tool, record count, and result. If a scan report contains credentials or exploit evidence, do not log the full payload. Store the raw evidence with access controls and keep the API request as a reference.

The token should also fail closed if its workspace is removed or its role is reduced. Test that behavior rather than assuming the next scheduled import will fail cleanly. A successful upload to the wrong workspace is harder to undo than a rejected request.

Use the 197 app matches as an inventory lead. For owned Faraday deployments, review users, roles, workspace assignments, token ownership, and API exposure. Test one import token against an unrelated workspace and one read token against a write operation.

Faraday helps a security team turn scanner output into a shared record of risk. The token model should preserve that record's boundaries. A workspace-scoped identity with a short, documented lifecycle is more useful than a long-lived administrator key that nobody can trace.

A Mute Button Must Fail Closed: Recording App and Microphone Audio with ReplayKit

📅 Nov 09, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

A screen recording can look correct while sounding completely wrong. A tutorial needs narration above the application’s audio; a demonstration may need only system sound. Treating both inputs as one volume control makes those two jobs unnecessarily difficult.

In Reel Screen Recorder, the broadcast extension receives ReplayKit buffers and keeps their source identity until the writer dispatches them. The useful engineering detail is not the slider itself. It is what happens when transforming a buffer fails.

The current SampleHandler.handleAudio distinguishes .audioMic from .audioApp and asks the shared settings for recordingGain(forMicrophone:). It then passes the processed buffer, with its original source type, to BroadcastWriter.

The writer routes video, app audio, and microphone audio to separate capture functions. Its asset writer uses the MP4 container. This division keeps capture policy out of the file-writing dispatch code and makes it possible to reason about each source independently.

The handler tries a volume-adjusted copy when gain is not one. If that transformation fails and the requested volume is zero, it tries to create a silent buffer. If silence creation also fails, it returns without writing the buffer.

Here is the policy in condensed pseudocode, not a drop-in ReplayKit implementation:

That last nonzero case is a fidelity compromise: requested gain may not be honored. The zero case is a privacy boundary. Writing the original buffer after the user muted it would violate the user’s explicit choice. The implementation treats those failures differently.

Reel also offers a picture-in-picture camera workflow. A camera preview, a system PiP surface, and an encoded recording are different objects with different lifetimes. A visible camera window alone is not proof that the exported clip contains the intended image and audio.

For verification, use a short recording with identifiable synthetic app sound and a spoken microphone marker. Inspect the exported MP4, then repeat with each source muted individually. Include interruption and failed-transform cases in tests. These are suggested checks, not performance results claimed from this code review.

The broader lesson is to preserve source identity until the last component that needs it. Mixing early makes later controls harder to explain and harder to verify. For the product workflow and alternatives, see Reel’s recording comparisons.

Available on Reel Screen Recorder on the App Store.

How to Compare AI Tools for Business With a Blind Test

📅 Nov 09, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Ask any dev team whether their AI coding tool is helping, and most will say yes. Ask them to prove it, and the room gets quiet.

That gap is real. In a randomized study published by METR in July 2025, 16 experienced open-source developers worked through 246 real tasks from their own repositories. Before starting, they expected AI to make them 24% faster. With AI allowed, they actually took 19% longer. Afterward, they still believed AI had sped them up by 20%.

That’s one study with early-2025 tools, and METR’s February 2026 update says developers are likely getting more of a speedup now. It also admits the newer data is too weak to say by how much. If a research team struggles to measure this, a gut feeling in a Slack thread won’t do better.

The 2025 Stack Overflow Developer Survey points the same way. 84% of respondents use or plan to use AI tools, yet more of them distrust the accuracy of the output (46%) than trust it (33%). The top frustration, named by 66%, is AI answers that are “almost right, but not quite.”

So when your manager asks you to compare AI tools for business use, don’t pick the one with the slickest demo. Build a small blind test instead. It takes about two weeks, two short Python scripts, and two honest reviewers.

Before testing anything, agree on what “better” means. For most teams it comes down to five things:

Quality on your own tasks, not on a leaderboard Fix time, or how long it takes to turn “almost right” into “done” Cost at your real usage, including caps and overages Fit with your editor, repo, CI, and sign-in setup Data handling, meaning where your code goes and whether it trains anyone’s model

Fix time is the one teams forget. A tool that writes 80% of a function in five seconds and leaves you twenty minutes of debugging isn’t saving anything.

Step 1: Build a task set from your own backlog

Pull eight to ten closed tickets where you already know what good looks like. Mix them up: a bug fix, a test-writing task, a refactor, some documentation, and at least one task in the oldest, messiest part of your codebase. That last one usually tells you the most.

Write the “done” criteria for each task before anyone runs a tool. If you write them afterward, you’ll grade on a curve without noticing.

Ten tools means ten half-finished tests. Pick three. That’s enough to see real differences without turning the test into a second job.

Your shortlist can come from teammates, vendor docs, community threads, or comparison directories when you know the job but not the product names. Compare Best AI is one of those. Compare Best AI helps businesses discover tools by use case, industry, and features. (Full disclosure: this post is published by the Compare Best AI team.) Treat any list as a starting point. Your own test makes the call.

Step 3: Run every task through every tool

Same prompt, same context, same person driving. Save each output in a folder per task, named after the tool:

outputs/ task-01/ tool-x.py tool-y.py tool-z.py

While you’re at it, log how many minutes each output takes to reach “done” in a plain text file. A phone timer is fine. Those minutes become your fix-time evidence in Step 5, and they’re much harder to argue with than a reviewer’s impression. If a tool needs several attempts before it gives a usable answer, count the retries too.

If reviewers know which tool wrote what, they’ll lean toward the one they already like. This script copies each output to a random letter and stores the answer key separately:

python import json, random, shutil, string from pathlib import Path

SRC, DST, KEYS = Path("outputs"), Path("blind"), Path("keys") KEYS.mkdir(exist_ok=True)

for task in sorted(p for p in SRC.iterdir() if p.is_dir()): files = list(task.iterdir()) random.shuffle(files) (DST / task.name).mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True) key = {} for label, f in zip(string.ascii_uppercase, files): shutil.copy(f, DST / task.name / f"{label}{f.suffix}") key[label] = f.stem # Reviewers never open keys/ until scoring is done (KEYS / f"{task.name}.json").write_text(json.dumps(key, indent=2))

Reviewers only open blind/. Nobody touches keys/ until every score is in.

Step 5: Score with weights you set in advance

Each reviewer scores quality and fix time from 1 to 5 on the blind files, where 5 is best (so a 5 for fix time means almost no cleanup). Cost, fit, and data handling don’t need blinding, so score those from your notes once the key is revealed. Everything goes into scores.csv:

reviewer,tool,quality,fix_time,cost,fit,data sam,tool-x,4,3,4,5,4 sam,tool-y,5,4,2,4,3 sam,tool-z,3,2,5,3,4 priya,tool-x,4,4,4,4,4 priya,tool-y,4,4,2,4,3 priya,tool-z,3,3,5,3,4

python import csv from collections import defaultdict

WEIGHTS = {"quality": 0.35, "fix_time": 0.25, "cost": 0.20, "fit": 0.10, "data": 0.10}

totals, counts = defaultdict(float), defaultdict(int) with open("scores.csv", newline="") as f: for row in csv.DictReader(f): totals[row["tool"]] += sum(float(row[k]) * w for k, w in WEIGHTS.items()) counts[row["tool"]] += 1

for tool in sorted(totals, key=lambda t: totals[t] / counts[t], reverse=True): print(f"{tool:<10} {totals[tool] / counts[tool]:.2f} / 5")

With that sample data, the output looks like this:

tool-x 3.92 / 5 tool-y 3.67 / 5 tool-z 3.38 / 5

Notice that tool-y had the best raw quality and still came second, mostly because it cost the most. That’s exactly the trade-off a polished demo hides.

One rule matters more than the code: lock the weights before anyone sees results. Tweaking them afterward until a favorite wins is the easiest way to ruin the whole test.

Step 6: Price a busy month, then check the exit

The sticker price is only the start. Ask what happens when you hit the usage cap, whether billing is per seat or per credit, and which features you tested sit behind a higher tier. Estimate a heavy month, not a quiet one.

Then check data and lock-in. Can you opt out of model training? Can admins control who has access? If you switch next year, can you export your prompts and settings? If your company wants a formal approach, the NIST AI Risk Management Framework is free and practical.

The best part of this setup is that you can reuse it. AI tools ship big updates every few months, so put a 90-day reminder on the calendar and re-run the same task set. The scripts and tickets already exist, so the second round takes a fraction of the time, and the next time someone asks you to compare AI tools for business, you’ll answer with numbers instead of vibes.

Tried something like this with your team? We’d like to hear which criteria ended up mattering most. Tell us in the comments.

Calciomercato no stop - Indiscrezioni, trattative e retroscena dell'8 novembre

📅 Nov 09, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

SASSUOLO, AQUILANI ORMAI ESONERATO. BOVE: “HO SEMPRE UN OCCHIO VERSO L’ITALIA” -   Una decisione sofferta ma ormai praticamente presa. Il futuro di Alberto Aquilani è sempre più lontano dal Sassuolo, a un passo dall’esonero. Gli ultimi risultati, 1 punto in 6 partite, hanno portato a imboccare una strada che un mese fa nessuno poteva immaginare visto il lavoro iniziato. Nemmeno lo stesso Aqullani, che anche se questa avventura finirà, resta un allenatore emergente con idee e spunti, gli stessi che aveva fatto vedere l’anno scorso nella splendida cavalcata con il Catanzaro. Un’annata con una promozione sfiorata di un soffio.

E dire che la sua prima avventura in serie A era cominciata bene, con la soddisfazione anche di una vittoria contro la Juventus e il pareggio con il Milan. Un Sassuolo con un’identità ben precisa. Dalla sconfitta con il Frosinone la squadra però non ha più saputo reagire denotando una grande fragilità, soprattutto a livello difensivo. Alla fine, come sempre, i risultati hanno fatto la differenza e portato la società ad andare verso un’altra direzione. Domani tutto sarà più chiaro con il Sassuolo che dovrà scegliere il sostituto. Tanti i nomi al vaglio del ds Palmieri: Paulo Sousa, Montella, D’Aversa e Calzona sono profili su cui si sta concentrando l’attenzione del club neroverde che nelle prossime ore farà la sua scelta definitiva.

"Ho scelto di raccontare la mia esperienza perché mi sono sentito fortunato, sono in vita e posso farlo, volevo ridare indietro l'affetto che ho ricevuto, trasformare le difficoltà in qualcosa di utile: vorrei che chiunque veda il documentario trovi energia per le proprie sfide personali, e anche il coraggio di farsi aiutare se necessario": così, parlando nel corso della puntata odierna de La Domenica Sportiva, Edoardo Bove, intervenuto nella nota trasmissione di Rai 2 per presentare il documentario "In un battito", che ripercorre la sua vita.

Segnata da un malore che ha messo a rischio la sua vita, quando era in campo. Ma è questo un ricordo passato: "Certo, gioco in maniera diversa adesso. Mi sono preparato durante questo lungo periodo al rientro in campo, sapevo che avrei trovato difficoltà a livello mentale anche nel rapporto con il mio cuore e il suo battito, a volte c'è la paura di ricordare; anche per questo è stato importante affrontare tutto, fare un percorso interiore che i aiutasse a gestire il tutto. In campo adesso sono tranquillo, ma la differenza l'ha fatta l'aver lavorato con dei professionisti che mi hanno permesso di superare tutto ciò: essere ora in Inghilterra mi rende anche felice, era comunque un sogno che avevo. Però non nascondo di avere un occhio verso l'Italia".

A tal proposito, una nota alla Roma: "Secondo me ha grandissime potenzialità, soprattutto nel campionato italiano in questo momento ha un ritmo molto elevato. Si difende attaccando, ha un gioco quasi asfissiante, a uomo: ho giocato contro Gasperini quando ero in Italia, ricordo bene il tipo di gioco e quanto mettesse in difficoltà gli avversari. Ma che è una Roma positiva non devo certo dirlo io".

Il futuro di Francesco Camarda potrebbe presto tingersi di una nuova maglia, seppur temporaneamente. Secondo quanto riportato oggi dal Corriere dello Sport, il Milan sta valutando con grande attenzione l'ipotesi di cedere in prestito il giovane centravanti durante la prossima sessione invernale di mercato, offrendogli così l'opportunità di trovare la continuità d'impiego necessaria per accelerare il suo percorso di crescita professionale.

Il classe 2008, chiuso nel ruolo di vice-Gonçalo Ramos all'interno della rosa rossonera, ha racimolato finora appena 6 presenze complessive tra campionato ed Europa League, per un minutaggio totale fermo a quota 119 minuti. Troppo poco spazio per mostrare la propria qualità, a discapito di quanto previsto inizialmente da Ruben Amorim nella scelta di tenerlo in rosa.

E quindi? La situazione di stallo ha indotto diversi club tra Serie A e Serie B a muoversi concretamente per sondare il terreno con l'entourage di Camarda, dal Frosinone al Monza, fino a Catanzaro e Pisa che si sono già informate sulla situazione del talento cresciuto nel vivaio del club di via Aldo Rossi. Sicuramente scaldare la panchina non è un'opzione per il resto della stagione.

MANCHESTER UNITED, CARRICK: “NON MI DIMETTERÒ”. MONACO, LIVERPOOL IN PRESSING PER CAMARA -  Il Manchester United continua a vivere una stagione complicatissima, ma Michael Carrick non ha alcuna intenzione di abbandonare la panchina dei Red Devils. Dopo il rocambolesco 4-4 contro l'Aston Villa, il tecnico inglese ha escluso categoricamente l'ipotesi di dimissioni, nonostante il deludente diciottesimo posto in Premier League. Interrogato sull'eventualità di lasciare il proprio incarico, Carrick ha risposto senza esitazioni ai microfoni di Sky Sports: "Non succederà".

L'allenatore, 45 anni, ha poi riconosciuto le difficoltà attraversate dalla squadra, capace di conquistare appena una vittoria nelle prime dieci giornate di campionato. "Conoscete la situazione, non abbiamo vinto abbastanza partite. Ma ho tutta l'intenzione di proseguire il lavoro che abbiamo iniziato, ripartendo dai tanti aspetti positivi mostrati oggi, correggendo alcuni errori e tornando a vincere".  Il futuro di Lamine Camara potrebbe essere lontano dal Monaco. Il centrocampista senegalese classe 2004, 22 anni, è diventato uno dei nomi più seguiti dai grandi club della Premier League, pronti a contenderselo nelle prossime sessioni di mercato. Secondo quanto riportato dal portale footy-africa.com, il Liverpool avrebbe intensificato i contatti con l'entourage del giocatore, nel tentativo di anticipare la concorrenza e capire i margini per una possibile operazione. L'interesse dei Reds rappresenta un elemento importante per il futuro del calciatore, anche se al momento non risultano accordi raggiunti con il Monaco.

Il Liverpool, tuttavia, non è l'unica società inglese sulle tracce del centrocampista. Anche Chelsea e Manchester United continuerebbero infatti a monitorarne la situazione, mantenendosi in corsa per un eventuale trasferimento. Una concorrenza che potrebbe rendere particolarmente movimentato il mercato attorno a Camara, considerato un profilo interessante per qualità tecniche, dinamismo e prospettive di crescita. Il Monaco si trova dunque a dover gestire l'attenzione crescente nei confronti di uno dei suoi giovani talenti, mentre dalla Premier League arrivano segnali di un interesse sempre più concreto. Per ora il Liverpool sembra essere il club più attivo nei contatti, ma la presenza di Chelsea e Manchester United lascia aperti diversi scenari. Il futuro di Camara potrebbe diventare uno dei temi più caldi delle prossime finestre di mercato.

After 30 Years, The Original Blue Ranger Officially Returned To Save The Power Rangers Universe

📅 Nov 09, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

During the original Mighty Morphin Power Rangers run, Tommy (Jason David Frank) and Jason (Austin St. John) took up the spotlight. Their rivalry, contrasting leadership styles, and status as the team’s most prominent fighters made them the focal points of the majority of the show’s ongoing arcs. Other Rangers had memorable stories, but rarely seemed quite as important as the Green and Red Ranger. That imbalance was particularly notable when it came to Billy (David Yost), the original Blue Ranger, whose intelligence repeatedly made everyone else’s victories possible.

Billy’s treatment became even harder to overlook in Power Rangers Zeo, which removed him from the active lineup and made him technical support. His expertise remained essential, yet the franchise made his role helping other Rangers rather than being a hero himself. Thankfully, 30 years after Billy made his debut in 1993, Netflix finally gave the original Blue Ranger the respect he’d always been lacking. Power Rangers Cosmic Fury and Mighty Morphin Power Rangers: Once And Always brought Billy back into action, recognizing that the team’s resident genius deserved far more than another season offering assistance from afar.

In Cosmic Fury, Billy reclaimed his Mighty Morphin Blue Ranger uniform while becoming pivotal to the final battle against Lord Zedd. What’s more, the Netflix special Power Rangers: Once And Always complemented that achievement by placing Billy’s brilliance and emotional struggles at the center of the plot. Together, these two late-stage Power Rangers stories proved what fans had always known: Billy had always been the original MMPR team’s backbone despite the fact that the show failed to acknowledge his importance.

When it first arrived, Billy’s return in Power Rangers Cosmic Fury was celebrated as an underappreciated MMPR hero finally receiving his due. Wearing his classic Blue Ranger suit and bringing the original Dinozords into the fight delivered obvious appeal for longtime fans of the franchise. However, the series gave him a role with greater importance than simply being nostalgia bait. Defeating Lord Zedd would have been impossible without his experience and scientific expertise. Billy quite literally saved the day.

How essential he was to the team's victory is why Billy’s presence in Power Rangers Cosmic Fury is one of the most rewarding returns to the show of any former Ranger. Rather than dropping in for a simple cameo, Billy is treated as a veteran who still thinks of himself as a hero with just as much responsibility to defend the universe from evil as the younger Rangers. Power Rangers Zeo effectively used Billy’s intelligence to justify him not having a place on the battlefield. Cosmic Fury recognized that the original Blue Ranger’s genius is exactly why he belongs on the frontlines.

What’s more, the return of Billy Cranston carries even greater significance in hindsight. Cosmic Fury remains the final live-action Power Rangers series released, and the subsequent Netflix and Disney+ projects were both intended to be full-scale reboots that reset the established timeline. While it may not have been designed as the final chapter in the Power Rangers saga, the story started by MMPR now ends with Cosmic Fury, making Billy’s importance in the final battle against Lord Zedd all the more poignant.

When David Yost returned to Power Rangers for Cosmic Fury, he stepped into a role that supplied the spiritual core of a farewell to a beloved TV series that had run for three decades. Having the original Blue Ranger help save the universe was perfect, as it was only fitting that the hero who spent so long supporting teammates who took the lion’s share of the glory got to carry the franchise’s farewell.

Power Rangers Cosmic Fury wasn’t the only moment David Yost’s Billy Cranston shined in 2023. Netflix also released the hour-long 30th anniversary special Power Rangers: Once and Always the same year. Much like Cosmic Fury, Once and Always helped rectify the narrative imbalance of the original MMPR run that saw Billy regularly sidelined by other characters. The original Mighty Morphin Power Rangers frequently made leadership struggles the center of its character drama, which meant Tommy and Jason were almost always the core focus. Billy rarely received comparable attention, despite being indispensable. The MMPR Black Ranger, Zack (Walter Emanuel Jones), faced similar problems despite being the most charismatic member of the team.

That imbalance explains why Mighty Morphin Power Rangers: Once And Always felt so refreshing for longtime fans of the franchise. Billy and Zack are the returning heroes around whom the special is built. Yes, Kat (Catherine Sutherland) and Rocky (Steve Cardenas) are there too, but it’s Zack and, especially, Billy who form the core of the story. Their prominence allows the anniversary celebration to revisit the original era from a different perspective, showing how much storytelling potential was wasted by keeping Jason and Tommy as the focus.

Billy benefits especially because his scientific work drives the story. Even though it has disastrous consequences, his attempt to recover Zordon through experiments involving the Morphin Grid demonstrates just how smart he truly is. Billy was never simply a tech-savvy teenager. He was an undisputed genius with an intellect large enough to shape and impact the entire Power Rangers universe. He’s capable of understanding and manipulating the fundamental forces behind Ranger powers, displaying talent far beyond the routine equipment repairs he was often seen carrying out in shows like Power Rangers Zeo.

The mistakes that lead to the arrival of Robo-Rita in Once and Always also give Billy’s intelligence huge emotional weight. His endeavours are driven by grief and loyalty rather than simple scientific curiosity, making him a compelling protagonist deserving of a much greater role than being a provider of convenient scientific solutions for his teammates. In both One and Always and Cosmic Fury, Billy’s brilliance is both celebrated and presented with the emotional nuance needed to make it a driving force for the wider narrative. Together, the two Netflix Power Rangers productions finally understand just how important the original Blue Ranger always was.

The disadvantages of buying tech from other countries

📅 Nov 09, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Importing tech isn't too hard, but the downsides may not be worth it.

There are many reasons to import phones or other tech, whether you're buying it in person during a trip or via online marketplaces or importers. Your phone could die while you're travelling abroad, for example, forcing you to buy a replacement. Or maybe there's a perfect product for you, but it's not available where you live. Either way, buying tech from other countries is sometimes the only option.

Depending on the product, though, this can come with a catch or two. Buying devices from other regions can come with annoying restrictions. You might end up with slightly different hardware, lack warranty coverage or, in the worst case, not have access to core software or connectivity features. Here's what you should know before buying a new phone, laptop or any other tech product from overseas.

A device can have different hardware configurations globally. In China, for example, certain iPhone models have slots for two physical SIMs. Conversely, US iPhones dropped the SIM tray starting with the iPhone 14, so you must use an eSIM instead. iPhones sold in China also get limited access to Apple Intelligence tools, even if you use it elsewhere.

Phones may pose issues with cellular bands. Manufacturers usually offer different versions of the same phone designed for specific bands in a given region. If you buy a phone in another country, it might not work on the US' 5G network, for example. This means you could lose access to carrier-specific features like VoLTE or Wi-Fi Calling if the device you're buying isn't certified for your network. It may seem minor, but it can be a headache if you plan to use it as your main device.

Other tech isn't free from potential issues, either. Laptops, for instance, can have different keyboard layouts depending on where you buy them. French laptops will use the AZERTY layout, and not every brand offers a QWERTY version in such markets. Depending on the product, power can be an issue too: imported products will likely use a different plug, so you'll need an adaptor, but that's not all. Watch out for voltages as well; the US uses 120V at 60Hz, but most of Europe and Asia use 220V to 240V, usually at 50Hz. Phone and laptop chargers usually work worldwide, but not all tech will.

Warranty is also an issue depending on what you're buying. Many manufacturers only provide tech support in the region where the device was sold. And if you're buying a product that isn't officially sold in the US, getting a broken part repaired can be tough, too. Some companies are more flexible when it comes to supporting devices purchased abroad, as is the case with AppleCare. Still, the company's warranty terms state that it may limit service for select devices, including the iPhone, to the country where the product was originally sold. Buying locally is still a safer bet if warranty is a priority.

If you're buying online, you also have to contend with import duties and shipping costs. The US has eliminated its $800 de minimis exemption for international parcels, so even small packages may get taxed quite heavily. International shipping isn't cheap, either. Most of the time, then, you have to really decide whether what you want to buy overseas is worth it.

Two Boy Meets World Stars Have A Spinoff Idea, And As A Fan, I Really Need It To Happen

📅 Nov 09, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Kayce Bringing Up Beth And Jamie On Marshals Gave Me An Idea For A Dutton Ranch Crossover

Are Fire Country And Sheriff Country Going THERE With Mickey And Manny? I'm Relieved Someone Asked Morena Baccarin

I Just Found Out Why Young Sheldon Ended After Seven Seasons

Unlock instant access to exclusive member features.

Get full access to premium articles, exclusive features and a growing list of member rewards.

Over a decade after Boy Meets World ended in 2000, a spinoff series was developed for Disney Channel and called Girl Meets World. But even after Girl ended in 2017, there were still some characters that fans wanted to see more of. That’s all the more reason I'm so excited to hear Will Friedle and Matthew Lawrence talking about their own spinoff idea. And, seriously, I need a network exec to hear them out!

Cory Matthews and Shawn Hunter may have been one of the most perfect best friend pairings, based on their exploits in Boy Meets World. However, those two characters' older brothers, Jack and Eric, are also truly a bromance deserving of attention. During a recent episode of Pod Meets World, Matthew Lawrence, who played Jack, said during a press junket that he was asked about a Jack/Eric spin-off series. Will Friedle dropped some very positive thoughts on the prospect, and they make me giddy:

I think you could still do it. Jack and Eric come together. It’s the new Odd Couple!

Yes! This needs to come together. For those who don’t know, Neil Simon’s play The Odd Couple is about two mismatched roommates, and Jack and Eric definitely fit that bill. While Jack is the logical straight man of the duo, Eric is the zany eccentric. As the two characters (and the actors who played) were so yin and yang, a spinoff would definitely make for some entertaining TV.

Lawrence went on to explain that a Jack/Eric spinoff was once in the cards, but the relationship between the creators and Disney “kind of fell apart.” Also, Lawrence recently told EW he could see the duo, both in their 40s, forced back together after going their separate ways. Lawrence continued to say on the podcast that if a potential spinoff isn’t picked up by television, he has an excellent idea of how a micro spin-off series could work, and I’m loving it:

If anything, it’s like the new YouTube in a sense that these days, as you see with Obsession and all of these things that are coming, I mean, that’s kind of the new groundlings when you talk about comedy. The new spot where they bring out shows. So maybe if we even start, maybe it’s like, build it and they will come.

It’s true that YouTube has become the new platform for creatives to bring their ideas to fruition before they hit the big or small screens. If there's enough attention, it would be so interesting to see if this possible BMW offshoot could hit streaming one day.

Friedle said himself he’s “totally down” for a spinoff, so I'm feeling somewhat hopeful that the two might try to see this through. The last time fans saw Jack’s big one-time return on Girl Meets World, he reunited with Eric. However, it was revealed that Jack was a high-ranking executive at an environmentally harmful firm, which clashed with Eric's position as a newly elected senator. However, the iconic duo mended fences at Eric’s niece’s school dance, with Jack admitting that his friendship with his old pal was “the best choice” he ever made.

This TV show idea seems better than a previously proposed spin-off idea that came while Boy Meets World was still airing. Friedle revealed he almost left the ABC/Disney sitcom when creator Michael Jacobs pitched a “young Friends” idea for Eric to move to L.A. and meet new friends there. While it’s always amusing to see Eric’s humorous interactions, I’d rather see a straight man/funny man dynamic between him and Jack.

Now that the idea of a Jack/Eric spinoff has been brought up, this Boy Meets World fan is keeping her fingers crossed that it comes to fruition. Given that mismatched roommate shows like The Big Bang Theory, 2 Broke Girls, Perfect Strangers have been successful, why not give Jack Hunter and Eric Matthews a shot?

In the meantime, fans can watch Eric and Jack's dynamic on Boy Meets World and Girl Meets World by streaming them with a Disney+ subscription.

Just your average South Floridian cinephile who believes the pen is mightier than the sword.

Bloom Energy Stock Fell 16% on the Same Day 2 Giant Data Center Deals Named It. Is It a Buy?

📅 Nov 09, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Only one of the two deals is new business. Is the stock's drop an opening?

Bloom Energy (BE +3.42%) turned up in two big data center announcements on Nov. 5. NTT DATA said it had agreed to deploy 900 megawatts of the fuel cell maker's onsite power across data center projects around the world.

And Alphabet's Google said Bloom would build the fuel cells for a 702-megawatt plant powering its first data center in Wyoming, a $17 billion project.

The stock had a bad day anyway. Shares ended at $167.52, off around 16%, amid worries that rising opposition to new data centers after the Nov. 3 midterms might slow the build-out of artificial intelligence (AI) data centers.

The growth stock is still up over 90% this year. But it now sits about 52% below the $345.85 record close it set on June 22.

How much new business did Bloom win on Nov. 5? Less than the headlines may suggest, because just one of the two deals is new. I think the other still matters, though, and the stock looks much more reasonably priced than it did in October.

Google won't buy the fuel cells itself. American Electric Power plans to invest around $4 billion in the 702-megawatt project near Cheyenne, Wyoming, the utility said on Oct. 14. It'll sell the plant's output to Google under a 20-year power purchase agreement, and it expects the plant to be fully running by late 2028.

The fuel cell order came months earlier. In January, an AEP subsidiary agreed to buy solid oxide fuel cells for around $2.65 billion for a plant near Cheyenne. The purchase used most of an option for 900 extra megawatts under AEP's 2024 supply deal with Bloom.

For a sense of scale, that one order is worth over half of Bloom's full-year revenue forecast of $4.4 billion to $4.6 billion for 2026.

So, Google's news didn't add to Bloom's orders. The fuel cells were bought in January, and AEP had already named Google as the plant's customer in October.

That still means something, I think. A fuel cell order backed by a 20-year power contract with Google looks more likely to become revenue on time than one linked to an unnamed buyer.

NTT DATA's deal is the new business, and the companies called it Bloom's biggest international project so far. NTT's data center arm plans to use the fuel cells as its main power source, with the relationship meant to reach gigawatt scale across its global portfolio.

What the release didn't say matters, too. It gave no dollar value, no project locations, and no timeline beyond first shipments starting in 2027. This deal shouldn't show up in Bloom's 2026 revenue, and Bloom hasn't said how much of it falls in 2027 or later.

But 900 megawatts is a lot of fuel cells -- nearly a full year of output at the 1 gigawatt of annual production capacity Bloom's Fremont, California, plant had going into 2026. The company planned to double that capacity by year-end.

Bloom needs deals like this, because its quarter-to-quarter growth slowed sharply in the third quarter. Second-quarter revenue of $1.07 billion was 42% above the first quarter's $751 million. Third-quarter revenue then climbed 16% sequentially to $1.24 billion, up 138% year over year. And October's raised outlook (management's third raise this year) implies a sequential gain of 9% to 25% in the fourth quarter.

That's still strong growth. Keeping it up into 2027 and 2028, though, depends on deals like NTT DATA's moving from announcement to shipment.

Based on the midpoint of management's adjusted earnings forecast for 2026 ($3.10 to $3.40 a share), Bloom stock cost around 52 times earnings at its Nov. 5 close. The same forecast had it at around 67 times earnings at the $217.77 close on Oct. 22. On next year's expected earnings, shares now trade at around 31 times earnings.

Still, 52 times earnings is a premium price. It assumes Bloom keeps growing quickly well past this year.

But Bloom landed a big new deal on Nov. 5 as its shares got much cheaper.

Of course, a slower U.S. data center build-out might delay some deliveries. And October's weak spot hasn't gone away. Bloom's operations burned $27 million in cash in the third quarter, mostly because it booked revenue faster than it could bill customers for it.

Is Bloom stock worth buying after a 16% one-day drop? I'd consider buying some shares at this price, a few at a time. And I'd maybe add more when billing catches up with revenue.

Daniel Sparks and his clients do not have positions in any of the stocks mentioned. The Motley Fool has positions in and recommends Alphabet and Bloom Energy. The Motley Fool has a disclosure policy.

10 Apple products and features that transformed under Tim Cook's leadership

📅 Nov 09, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Apple changed a lot in the last 15 years.

When Tim Cook became Apple's CEO in 2011, he began leading what was already one of the world's most successful technology companies. The iPhone was driving Apple's growth, the iPad was still relatively new and the Mac was at the center of its computing business. Cook inherited an enormous ecosystem, then he spent 15 years finding new ways it could be put to use.

By the end of Cook's tenure, Apple had introduced entirely new product categories, moved the Mac to its own processors, turned the iPhone into a wallet and satellite communicator and built a services business worth more than $100 billion a year. Meanwhile, some of Apple's biggest changes weren't new products, but rather familiar ones that slowly evolved into something else.

There are now more than 2.5 billion active Apple devices, while the company brought in $416.2 billion in net sales in fiscal 2025. Apple's announcement on Cook's transition lays out the scale of the company he leaves behind. But the more interesting story is how under his leadership, Apple products took on entirely new forms and jobs.

When the Apple Watch launched in 2015, it was primarily an iPhone companion for notifications, communication, apps and activity tracking. Apple began expanding its health and fitness capabilities with Apple Watch Series 2, which added built-in GPS, swim tracking and more workout features.

The bigger shift came with Apple Watch Series 4 in 2018. With this, Apple added an ECG app and fall detection, giving the Watch capabilities that went beyond basics like tracking steps. The ECG could record a heart rhythm directly from the wrist, while fall detection could automatically contact emergency services if the wearer appeared to have taken a hard fall and remained immobile.

The watchOS 11 update in 2024 added sleep apnea notifications, the Vitals app and Training Load, giving you more ways to track and interpret health and fitness data. In 2025, the Apple Watch Series 11 introduced sleep scoring and hypertension notifications. While the watch started as an accessory for your iPhone, it now monitors a whole suite of metrics that the iPhone can't.

AirPods could have been just a pair of wireless earbuds for your iPhone, but Apple kept giving them more reasons to exist within the larger Apple ecosystem. Automatic pairing, seamless switching between devices, active noise cancellation and spatial audio made them an attractive option for all your music and other audio.

Apple has given AirPods an important role beyond entertainment, though. Starting with AirPods Pro 2, Apple added a scientifically validated Hearing Test, Hearing Aid functionality for people with mild to moderate hearing loss and Hearing Protection. These hearing health tools make support more accessible to lots of people. Meanwhile, the AirPods Pro 3 added heart rate sensing to track your workout effort with the buds you're likely already wearing while exercising.

Under Cook, Apple took a familiar product and expanded what it could do, without asking people to adopt an entirely new device.

For years, Apple built its own chips for the iPhone, iPad and Apple Watch while relying on Intel processors for the Mac. That changed in 2020, when Apple announced a two-year transition to its own silicon. The move gave Mac computers a common architecture with Apple's other products, providing developers with a more unified platform for building software across the ecosystem.

The first Apple silicon Macs arrived later that year. Apple put the M1 chip in the MacBook Air, 13-inch MacBook Pro and Mac mini, combining the CPU, GPU, Neural Engine and other components into a single system-on-a-chip. This helped Apple push the Mac toward the same performance and power efficiency that had come to define its mobile devices.

The difference between Apple's processors and Intel's isn't just speed. After Apple took over this core piece of Mac hardware, it had holistic control of the hardware design, chips inside and macOS itself. This helped the Mac overcome its midlife crisis.

What started as a convenient way to snap a picture became a sophisticated camera system with multiple lenses, computational photography, Night mode and some serious video capabilities.

The iPhone 11 was a big turning point. It featured an Ultra Wide camera, Night mode and Deep Fusion, which uses machine learning to process photos pixel by pixel for more detail and less noise. The iPhone 12 Pro then added Apple ProRAW, combining Apple's computational photography with the flexibility of a RAW file for those who wanted more control over their images.

Apple's more recent iPhones have continued this progress. The iPhone 15 Pro brought a 48MP main camera, multiple focal-length options, 48MP ProRAW and professional video features including ProRes recording to external storage. Our iPhone 15 review highlighted the camera as a major part of the upgrade. Later, our iPhone 17 review covered more camera improvements, including a new Center Stage front camera and upgraded rear cameras. For many people, their iPhone camera is the way they take pictures, and what all other smartphone cameras are judged against.

Apple Pay is easy to take for granted because it feels so normal now. But in 2014, putting a payment system directly into the iPhone people already carried everywhere gave the device a new role: replacing the cards in their wallet. Apple Pay eventually expanded beyond in-store purchases to apps, websites and other Apple devices like the Apple Watch.

10 years after its launch, the company stated that Apple Pay was used by hundreds of millions of people across 78 regions, at millions of websites and apps and tens of millions of stores. It was also supported by more than 11,000 banks and "network partners" at that milestone. Apple Pay later expanded to third-party iOS and desktop browsers, providing more ways to check out online without typing your card details into every site.

That idea of leaving your physical wallet behind has grown beyond payments, too. Wallet can hold other items like event tickets, transit cards, keys and even eligible IDs. In 2025, Apple started expanding its Digital ID for use with participating US states, plus support for Japan's My Number Card. Expanded Apple Wallet ticket features have also added venue maps, parking directions and other event information.

Some of the iPhone's biggest changes under Cook had more to do with connectivity than the phone itself, as Apple kept expanding the ways your iPhone can connect to the world around it. The iPhone 12 brought 5G to Apple's phones in 2020, while MagSafe introduced a ring of magnets that made wireless charging easier and created an all-new suite of snap-on accessories.

The iPhone 14 added something even more ambitious: direct satellite connections to message emergency services when cellular and Wi-Fi coverage isn't available. Apple also added the ability to share a location through Find My via satellite. In 2024, satellite connectivity was expanded to support Messages via satellite for messaging friends when you have no other options.

Not everything that changed under Tim Cook came in a box. Apple's services business grew from a relatively small part of the company into a $109.2 billion business, making it a core part of Apple's revenue alongside the iPhone.

The category is broader than just subscriptions. It includes the App Store, advertising, cloud services, Apple Music, Apple TV, Apple Pay and more. In 2025, the App Store alone averaged more than 850 million weekly users, while Apple said developers had earned more than $550 billion through the platform since 2008. Apple's services revenue also hit a quarterly record in early 2026, topping $30 billion.

Hardware still accounts for most of Apple's revenue, but the company's various services provide a huge business built around what people do after they buy an Apple device.

Apple had been selling digital content long before the Apple TV streaming service (formerly called Apple TV+) arrived in 2019. But with it, the company was no longer just offering access to content made by others. Apple TV+ launched with an original catalog of shows, movies and documentaries, including The Morning Show, See and For All Mankind.

Apple quickly built a reputation around those originals. CODA became the first streaming film to win the Academy Award for Best Picture in 2022, while shows like Ted Lasso and Severance became some of the service's biggest hits.

Apple TV+ earned a record 81 Emmy nominations across 14 Apple Original titles in 2025, led by Severance with 27 nominations and The Studio with 23. Both shows also took home awards that year. By September 2025, Apple said its originals had accumulated 620 wins and 2,816 award nominations.

Vision Pro is different from almost everything else Apple released under Cook. Instead of putting another screen in front of you, Apple built a computer that adds your digital life into the space around you. When Apple unveiled Vision Pro in 2023, it called the device its first "spatial computer," with visionOS and an interface controlled by your eyes, hands and voice.

What may be more interesting than the $3,499 headset is where Apple thinks computing could go next. Apps can float around a room instead of being confined to a traditional display, which provides immersive environments with three-dimensional content. More than 600 apps and games built specifically for Vision Pro were available when it launched.

As our M5 Vision Pro review found, the device is unlikely to become the next big Apple platform unless future revisions make it more accessible. But that's why it's worth a mention here: Vision Pro is Apple trying to create a new platform instead of solely relying on what's worked in the past.

The premise of an AirTag is simple: attach one to something you don't want to lose and your iPhone will help you find it. Its bigger innovation is how the tracker taps into Apple's existing Find My network, which uses nearby Apple devices to detect an AirTag and anonymously report its approximate location to its owner. An AirTag doesn't need its own cellular connection or GPS to be useful.

Apple describes AirTag as "the bestselling item finder", and continues expanding what it can do. The second-generation AirTag, released in 2026, added a longer Bluetooth range and more precise finding. Apple's Share Item Location feature also lets you securely share an item's location with participating airlines to help recover lost luggage.

The second-generation AirTag's primary usefulness comes from Apple's enormous network of devices, giving it access to a vast web to help locate a lost item.

Ramayana Producer On How Being A ‘Fanboy’ Of Chris Nolan Films Inspired One Thing About The Epic

📅 Nov 09, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Someone Asked Timothèe Chalamet How He Feels About Dune’s Ending (And Now I Can't Wait)

Someone Finally Asked Matt Damon When He Knew The Odyssey Would Be A Hit. His Answer Was Really Satisfying

The Indian movie features one awesome collab.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

India’s most expensive movie ever is Ramayana, which is one of the latest 2026 movie releases to hit theaters. The movie was a massive undertaking, and that's evident just by watching the trailer. All in all, the film is firmly steeped in the culture its representing. But, even though the flick was made thousands of miles from Hollywood, a producer was very much inspired by one of our greatest filmmakers, Christopher Nolan, as CinemaBlend recently learned.

Did you know that Ramayana is co-composed by Hans Zimmer? The German musician has become one of the most beloved and prolific composers of modern cinema, and that’s exactly why the movie's producer, Namit Malhotra, asked for him to come aboard the filmmaking team. Here’s how he told us the story during CB’s exclusive Ramayana interview:

I grew up in India completely fanboying on A.R. Rahman, who I absolutely love and his music has sort of formed a big part of my growing up and childhood and youth. And then, I moved to L.A. in 2010, the year Inception came out. I started to fall in love with Chris Nolan and Han Zimmer's music that was done for The Dark Knight and all the films that came subsequent to that. And, it was one of those moments when we decided to make Ramayana. I said literally as a fanboy, ‘What if I could take Han Zimmer and A.R. Rahman together and they would create a new sound, a new feeling, a new energy that the world has not heard before?’ And everybody is like, ‘Yeah, why not?’

Malhotra called himself a self-described “fanboy” of Nolan movies who's been in awe of his movies for a while. So, when he went on to make his own epic, he thought he'd take the long-shot move to try to get Zimmer on board for Ramayana alongside his favorite composer from India, A.R. Rahman. (Did you know Zimmer actually helped convince Nolan to make Interstellar, by the way? A greatly overlooked detail is just how important musicians like him are pivotal to the filmmaking process.) As Malhotra added:

One thing I learned in America, there's no harm in asking because the worst you'll be told is ‘No’. That's not how we grew up in India. Like you normally don't ask the question if you think the answer is no.

Even though Namit Malhotra had never met Zimmer and didn't know would react to the request, the producer took “a cue from [his] American friends” and reached out to the Interstellar composer. This is what happened next:

I made a pitch and a presentation to him. I couldn't have been more surprised by his intellect, his intuitive judgment because he was like, ‘Namit, you don't have to sell me on Ramayana. If something has existed in the world for over 5,000 years and generation after generation, more people are excited to consume it, learn from it, and it's still not lost its relevance, there must be more than what you and I can understand. So we should just figure out how we do this and not worry about whether we should do it.’

Ramayana Is Making A Huge Global Push. I Still Can't Tell From Reviews What I'm Getting Into

The idea really does sell itself, doesn’t it? Ramayana is a Hindu epic poem that’s very central to India’s culture. It has inspired the celebrations of Diwali and Dussehra, and the story also serves as a moral cornerstone as well. It focuses on two characters: Rama and Ravana. Prince Rama is the seventh incarnation / avatar of Lord Vishnu, who becomes responsible for taking down King Ravana from having uncontrollable power over people. It's India's own classic good vs. evil story, and it has really stood the test of time.

I'm no sure how aware Zimmer was of the legend of Ramayana before it was pitched to him, yet he was apparently sold quite quickly. He got the importance of it being told for the big screen. Malhotra told us that Zimmer’s reaction to his pitch even “revalidated” his own reasons to make it and “reinvigorated [his] own confidence” since he respects the composer so much.

Hans Zimmer is a busy musical savant, who’s recently worked on the Dune movies and the upcoming Harry Potter TV series, so he could have easily passed, but he clearly didn’t want to. And, as a result of his participation, one of Hollywood's best got to put his mark on a historical Indian film. You can hear Zimmer’s original music for Ramayana, which is playing in theaters around the world right now.

Sarah El-Mahmoud has been with CinemaBlend since 2018 after graduating from Cal State Fullerton with a degree in Journalism. In college, she was the Managing Editor of the award-winning college paper, The Daily Titan, where she specialized in writing/editing long-form features, profiles and arts & entertainment coverage, including her first run-in with movie reporting, with a phone interview with Guillermo del Toro for Best Picture winner, The Shape of Water. Now she's into covering YA television and movies, and plenty of horror. Word webslinger. All her writing should be read in Sarah Connor’s Terminator 2 voice over.

That’s $1,200 in credits you can reap in year 1 if you’re approved now.

The list of Splurge Credit merchants is 1stDibs, American Airlines (exclusions apply), Best Buy®, Future Personal Training, and Live Nation (exclusions apply) and you have to activate up to 2 merchants at a time prior to purchase, though you can change your selection as you wish. The Blacklane credit is actually $100 January – June and $100 July – December.

It offers a Priority Pass Select Membership (as with other major issuers for most cards, this does not cover airport restaurants). It includes 2 guests. Authorized cardmembers receive Priority Pass as well. And there’s also four American Airlines Admirals Club passes each year.

Capital One Venture X Rewards Credit Card lets you transfer your miles to your choice of 15+ travel loyalty programs, and offers a premium rewards card at a lower annual fee than several other cards in its class. (See rates and fees)

It has an offer to earn 75,000 bonus miles when you spend $4,000 on purchases in the first 3 months from account opening, equal to $750 in travel

And it’s a strong card for ongoing spend with unlimited 10X miles on hotels and rental cars booked through Capital One Travel and 5X miles on flights and vacation rentals booked through Capital One Travel, plus unlimited 2X miles on all other purchases

The card offers a $300 annual credit for bookings through Capital One Travel, where you’ll get Capital One’s best prices on thousands of trip options. And get 10,000 bonus miles (equal to $100 towards travel) every year, starting on your first anniversary. Enjoy access to 1,300+ lounges worldwide, including Capital One Lounge and Landing locations and participating Priority PassTM lounges, after enrollment.

The Southwest Rapid Rewards® Priority Credit Card (See rates and fees) offers 90,000 bonus points after spending $3,000 on purchases in the first 3 months from account opening. It has a $229 annual fee.

The Southwest Rapid Rewards® Plus Credit Card (See rates and fees) offers 80,000 bonus points after spending $1,000 on purchases in the first 3 months from account opening. Its annual fee is $99. That’s a much lower spending requirement for a bonus that’s only 10,000 points smaller.

Southwest’s Companion Pass lets someone fly with you for just taxes and fees, even when you redeem points for your own ticket. For someone who flies Southwest with a partner a few times a year, it’s the single best deal in domestic air travel. And the easiest way to earn it is about to disappear.

There’s still a final window to use an 80,000- or 90,000-point credit card initial bonus toward a pass valid through December 31, 2027. You need to apply by December 2, 2026 and get approved.

I’d choose Plus for the lower cost of getting the bonus. Priority costs $130 more per year, but gives you better seat benefits, 4,500 more anniversary points and a larger initial bonus. For a family that flies Southwest regularly, those seating benefits can easily be worth the difference.

Bilt Palladium Card (See rates and fees) has a strong offer of $300 in Bilt Cash on approval plus 50,000 points after $4,000 in non-housing spend in the first 3 months.

When you earn the initial bonus you also receive Bilt Rewards Gold elite status valid for the rest of the year and the entire next year. That unlocks early access to events and special activities, higher transfer bonuses, and preserves 1:1 transfers from Rakuten (they’ve only promised that for all members for six months). It also unlocks access to Bilt’s Home Away From Home program which you might think of as similar to American Express Fine Hotels & Resorts for their Platinum cardmembers. Palladium comes with a Priority Pass that includes two complimentary guests.

The card earns 2 Bilt Points per dollar on everyday spend. And you can choose to earn 4% back in Bilt Cash as well, which unlocks point earnings on rent and mortgage payments with no transaction fee (as above, spending 75% of your housing payment lets you earn as many additional points as you spend on rent and mortgage). And you can redeem Bilt Cash to earn an additional 1 point per dollar spent up to $5,000 in spend, up to five times per year. Up to $100 of Bilt Cash earned rolls over to the next year.

That means you can earn 3.3 points per dollar spent up to the amount of your housing payment, and 3 points per dollar spent on up to $25,000 after that. And I consider Bilt to have the most valuable points, with the most and best points transfer partners and the ability to redeem points at 1.25 cents apiece against the cost of travel through their portal.

The Brand New IHG One Rewards Premier Select Credit Card (See rates and fees) is Chase’s new $350 annual fee IHG credit card can return $400 in flight credits before your first renewal, on top of a 200,000-point initial bonus. It also gives you enough elite nights to choose a confirmable suite upgrade without first earning those nights through hotel stays.

The launch offer lets you earn 200,000 IHG points after $5,000 in purchases within three months of account opening. At my half-cent valuation, the 200,000-point bonus is worth $1,000.

The card offers a $200 airline statement credit per calendar year through December 31, 2027. It takes a single purchase of at least $250 in flight tickets booked directly with an airline.

Open the card this fall, make a qualifying purchase in 2026, and make another in 2027 before your first anniversary. That’s $400 back against airfare you’d otherwise buy, compared with a $350 first-year fee. Additional key benefits include:

Up to $300 a year in IHG Food & Beverage Rewards. You get one $75 voucher each quarter. It expires at the end of the quarter, must be applied to eligible charges on your room folio during a participating stay, and any unused balance disappears when you redeem it (e.g. use just $45 and you lose the other $30).

Every fourth night free on qualifying points stays. This applies to consecutive standard room award nights at the same hotel. Certificates don’t substitute for the points nights needed to trigger it.

Automatic Platinum status. That brings benefits including upgrades subject to availability, but it doesn’t include Diamond’s breakfast welcome amenity choice.

Up to $50 in United TravelBank cash. Register to receive $25 in each half of the year. The first-half deposit expires July 15 and the second-half deposit expires January 15 of the following year.

The World Of Hyatt Credit Card (See rates and fees) has a new initial bonus: earn 4 free nights at any Category 1-4 Hyatt hotel or resort after spending $5,000 on purchases in the first 3 months from account opening. That’s an improved offer from a card whose bonuses have usually been more modest than competing travel cards.

I have this card, and I put actual spending on it. Hyatt points are still valuable, but what gets me to use the card is the credit toward elite status and the rewards earned along the way. Hyatt status does more to improve my hotel stays than status with other major chains.

These are four separate certificates, so you can use them for one trip or spread them across different stays. You’ll need standard room award availability, and the certificates expire 12 months after they’re issued. The terms require checkout before the expiration date. A Category 4 standard room can cost as much as 25,000 points per night. Four certificates could therefore cover nights that would otherwise require 100,000 points. Their category restrictions and expiration date make them less flexible than having those points in your account.

The annual Category 1-4 free night after each cardmember anniversary makes the $95 fee easy to justify. There’s also a second Category 1-4 night after $15,000 in purchases during a calendar year which makes that spending more rewarding. And these nights are transferable to another World of Hyatt member

More significantly, it comes with 5 qualifying nights each year, plus 2 more qualifying nights for every $5,000 spent. Those nights count toward both status and Milestone Rewards. Put $15,000 of otherwise unbonused spending on the card during a calendar year and you earn 15,000 Hyatt points; an additional Category 1-4 free night certificate; 6 qualifying nights from spending, giving you 11 including the card’s annual 5.

Could Buying Palantir Stock Today Set You Up for Life?

📅 Nov 09, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The company's strong leadership in AI software has propelled the stock to life-changing returns since late 2020.

Palantir Technologies (PLTR +3.48%) has already changed lives for investors who bought in early. Since the company's direct listing in 2020, shares have soared by more than 1,500%. That would have turned $10,000 into roughly $162,000 in roughly five years.

But can buying Palantir stock now still set you up for life? Unfortunately, math is not on your side. Despite the company's blistering growth, the stock's next 1,500% will probably take far longer. Here's why.

Few companies can match the explosive growth Palantir is achieving. It generated just over $2.3 billion in total revenue in the third quarter of 2026, growing 96% year over year and 20% over the prior quarter. Palantir has been posting eye-popping growth for a few years now, which is how the stock has produced such high returns.

That success has already made Palantir, at a market cap of $412 billion, one of the world's largest companies. It's a hefty valuation for a company of Palantir's revenue base, even with that rampant growth rate. At its current price, shares trade at 55 times sales, making it one of Wall Street's more expensive names.

But even if Palantir sustains that valuation, it's going to be difficult for the stock to continue rising as it has. Even another 100% will push it to near a $1 trillion market cap. There aren't many stocks worth $1 trillion or more, and those that are all generate significantly more revenue.

So it's unrealistic to expect Palantir to rise another 1,500% anytime soon. It will probably take years of prolonged success.

Of course, Palantir can still set you up for life with returns of 100% to 300%. The catch is that you need to invest a bit more than you would for a stock that grows 1,500%. That leads to the question of whether it's wise to risk an enormous capital investment in just one stock.

Such a large investment is difficult for most diversified portfolios. It's probably wiser to lower your expectations for Palantir stock than to take a big risk by putting too much of your portfolio into one company.

The artificial intelligence (AI) trade has eroded in the early sessions following the recent midterm elections, in which Democrats regained control of the Senate and the House of Representatives. This shift could lead to a more aggressive regulatory stance toward the AI industry.

An extended sell-off would let a bit of air out of the stock. If that happens, long-term investors who believe in the company's ongoing momentum can recapture some of the stock's upside at more attractive entry prices.

Justin Pope has no position in any of the stocks mentioned. The Motley Fool has positions in and recommends Palantir Technologies. The Motley Fool has a disclosure policy.
