In line with expectations, new customers added in the last 12 months drove 48% of the net new subscription revenue added in Q3. As of quarter end, current remaining performance obligations reached $818 million, up 17% year-over-year. That represents the subscription revenue we expect to recognize over the next 12 months, and the growth rate includes an approximately 1 percentage point headwind due to foreign currency. On the services side, professional services revenue was $17 million, up 15% versus the prior year, driven by outperformance in XBRL services. Turning to profitability and operating performance. Our non-GAAP operating margin for the third quarter was 18.3%.

This exceeded the high end of our guidance by 85 basis points and was a 560 basis point improvement over Q3 2025. Our margin expansion was driven by top-line outperformance and continued operational discipline, including productivity gains across the business. Moving down the P&L to net income and earnings per share. Beginning in Q3, we are applying an effective tax rate of 22% to our non-GAAP results, reflecting our sustained profitability. In prior quarters, our non-GAAP results incorporated our GAAP tax expense, which was relatively nominal. On this new basis, non-GAAP net income was $41 million, up 55% year-over-year, and non-GAAP net income per diluted share was $0.72, up from $0.46 in Q3 last year.

Our prior guidance did not assume this 22% tax rate. To help with your models, our press release and investor presentation recast prior quarters at the new rate. This change affects non-GAAP presentation only. It has no impact on actual cash taxes, cash flow, or our GAAP results. On a GAAP basis, net income for the quarter was $19 million, up from $3 million in Q3 last year. GAAP net income per diluted share was $0.35, up from $0.05 a year ago and $0.10 above the high end of our guidance range. Moving on to our performance metrics for the quarter. We closed Q3 with 6,840 customers, representing a net increase of 299 customers compared to the third quarter 2025.

Our gross retention rate remained 97%, once again exceeding our 96% target. Our net retention rate for the quarter was 110%. Consistent with our top-line results, we experienced a modest headwind from foreign currency. Our multi-solution expansion strategy also continues to show up in our revenue mix. In Q3, customers with multiple solutions represented 77% of subscription revenue, up from 73% in Q3 2025. We also saw continued strength across our large contract customer cohorts. As of the end of the third quarter, we had 2,853 contracts valued at over $100,000 per year, up 20% year over year. Contracts valued at over $300,000 totaled 708, up 31% over the prior year.

Contracts valued at over $500,000 reached 283, up 20% from Q3 2025. Next, let's review our balance sheet and cash flows. As of September 30th, 2026, cash equivalents, and marketable securities were $817 million, an increase of $2 million over the second quarter. The primary use of cash this quarter was a settlement of our convertible notes due in August for $71 million in cash. Additionally, we repurchased 32,624 shares of our Class A common stock for $2 million. That's a more measurable pace than recent quarters as we prioritize the paydown of our convertible debt obligation.

As of quarter end, we have repurchased a total of $246 million under our $350 million share repurchase program, with $104 million remaining as we entered the fourth quarter. Moving on to guidance. Our guidance assumes a slight year-over-year FX headwind to projected revenue growth in Q4. With that in mind, for the fourth quarter of 2026, we expect total revenue to range from $275 million-$278 million. We expect services revenue to be up slightly compared to Q4 2025, and we expect non-GAAP operating margin to be in the range of 19%-19.5%. For the full year 2026, we expect total revenue to range from $1.041 billion-$1.044 billion.

We continue to expect subscription revenue to grow approximately 19% year over year, and we expect total services revenue to be up moderately year-over-year. We are raising our non-GAAP operating margin outlook by 25 basis points at the midpoint compared to our prior guidance, and now expect it to be in the range of 18.2%-18.3%. At the midpoint, this represents an 835 basis point improvement year-over-year, reflecting our continued focus on driving operating leverage as we scale. Finally, we continue to expect our free cash flow margin to be approximately 21% for the year. To conclude, our third quarter results reflect another quarter of disciplined execution against our plan.

As we close out 2026, our team is focused on capturing our Q4 opportunity, which is seasonally our largest bookings quarter, and delivering on our full year outlook. We look forward to sharing our formal 2027 outlook on our next call in February. As I shared at our Investor Day in September, top line growth and expanding profitability are not competing priorities at Workiva. They reinforce each other. We will end 2026 as a profitable billion-dollar platform, confident in our multi-year trajectory and path toward our 2030 targets. With that, I will turn the call over to the operator for Q&A.

FIGS (FIGS) Q3 2026 Earnings Call Transcript

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Guidance rose as international sales neared a fifth of the business.

Need a quote from a Motley Fool analyst? Email [email protected]

FIGS, Inc. (FIGS -7.89%) reported its fourth consecutive quarter of revenue growth exceeding 25%, leading management to raise full-year 2026 guidance for net revenue and margins. The company experienced an acceleration in active customer growth and average order value, while its international segment expanded to nearly 20% of the total business mix. Management indicated that while supply chain transitions from Jordan to Southeast Asia continue to impact inventory levels, core stock levels are improving in the fourth quarter. The company also expanded its non-scrubwear category through the acquisition of V Coterie and the launch of jewelry and accessories under the CHARxMACY brand.

Operator: We will now hand the conference over to Tom Shaw, Senior Vice President of Investor Relations. Please go ahead.

Tom Shaw: Good afternoon, and thank you for joining us to discuss FIGS' third quarter 2026 results, which we released this afternoon and can be found in our earnings press release and in the shareholder presentation posted to our investor relations website at ir.wearFIGS.com. Presenting on today's call are Trina Spear, our Co-Founder and Chief Executive Officer, and Sarah Oughtred, our Chief Financial Officer. As a reminder, remarks on this call that do not concern past events are forward-looking statements. These may include predictions, expectations, or estimates, including about future financial performance, market opportunity, or business plans. Forward-looking statements include risk and uncertainties, and actual results could differ materially.

These and other risks are discussed in our SEC filings, including in the 10-Q we filed today. Do not place undue reliance on forward-looking statements, which speak only as of today, and which we undertake no obligation to update. Finally, we will discuss certain non-GAAP metrics and key performance indicators, which we believe are useful supplemental measures for understanding our business. Definitions and reconciliations of these non-GAAP measures to their most comparable GAAP measures are included in our shareholder presentation. Now, I would like to turn the call over to Trina.

Trina Spear: Thanks, Tom. Good afternoon, everyone, and thank you for joining us. FIGS delivered another remarkable quarter, exceeding expectations on both the top and bottom line. Net revenues grew 26% to approximately $191 million, well ahead of our 20% outlook and marking our fourth straight quarter of greater than 25% growth. Strength was once again broad-based across categories, channels, and geographies. Our customer data was highlighted by an acceleration in year-over-year growth across three key measures. This included active customer growth of 14% to nearly 3.2 million, average order value growth of 11% to $126, and net revenues per active customer growth of 13% to another FIGS record of $236.

In addition to our top-line success, we also delivered increased profitability despite the elevated overall cost environment. Our adjusted EBITDA margin expanded to 16.5%, up 410 basis points year-over-year, surpassing our outlook of approximately 14%. Gross margin remained incredibly strong at over 69%, and we saw greater than planned SG&A leverage, which again highlights the power of our model as we scale. We continue to put capital to work as we repurchased approximately $14 million of our shares during the period, following the additional $100 million buyback authorization announced in August. We have now delivered four amazing quarters in a row.

Over this time, the consistent question we have received has been, how do we drive this sharp inflection in the business? The simple answer is that it has been through the combined efforts of great products, marketing campaigns that resonate deeply, a community that we are incredibly connected with, and strong industry fundamentals. But that simplicity understates the reality. Executing on all of this was not easy. It required a step change across a range of areas where we had always been successful, but where we knew we had to get even better.

At the heart of this work is the amazing team we have assembled over the past several years, with strong leaders and support fortifying our efforts across product, marketing, supply chain, finance, international teams, and Community Hubs. Their contributions and energy have been pivotal in unlocking this powerful story that has been there all along. On the product side, we have always had category-defining core offerings. What has changed is the sophistication we have added to every part of our go-to-market approach, which really began to take hold in the second half of 2025.

We are now more aligned than ever in how we understand and dictate style, deliver impactful color, improve fit, diversify our fabrics, enhance our merchandising, and invest in inventory across both core and limited edition. On the brand side, we renewed our focus on the top of the funnel, anchored by last year's "Where Do You Wear FIGS?" campaign and extending this year with "Never Change." We leveraged key viral moments from the Olympics to Noah Lyles at the Emmys, magnified our community support through expanded advocacy, and built greater sophistication across the full funnel.

This collective impact is real and differentiated, and we have powered these efforts in product and brand to serve an industry that has become increasingly rare in today's landscape. In addition to the replenishment-driven and essential nature of healthcare apparel, the job backdrop is strong, with healthcare-related jobs powering the economy. This is predicted to remain true going far into the future. Indeed, even amid fears of AI disruption, the Bureau of Labor Statistics projects the sector will add the most jobs over the next decade, about 37% of all new jobs. This is a fundamentally human profession, and we are proud to have built an authentic community around it.

Just as we know this industry is incredibly strong, being successful depends on creating strong human connections to the healthcare professionals who power it. We have seen a number of longstanding players in the industry fall on hard times at the same time we have accelerated, and we believe it is our ability to drive product innovation, impact, and community connection that puts us on a different path. Even with this run of strong performance, we are focused on what is next, sustaining our momentum and driving growth over the long run. Importantly, we see our recent efforts and success as foundational.

They were never designed to simply be one-time wins, but rather sustainable building blocks that drive enduring impact for healthcare professionals. Great foundations enable further progress, and we are setting our sights across a multitude of opportunities to raise the high bar we have set again. For instance, in product, our enhanced design capabilities are just now starting to have a meaningful impact. Our inventory positioning and depth is set to improve and be optimized, and we are accelerating our head-to-toe category expansion, thereby creating TAM. As we think about our brand's reach, we are far from the finish line as we look to serve more of the 100 million plus global healthcare community.

Domestically, we still have relatively low brand awareness, a digital experience that can become more personalized and engaging, and omnichannel connectivity that is untapped. Internationally, we are still under 20% of our total mix, with a number of key growth markets still in early stages. Physical in-person access to our brand remains highly limited, with just nine Community Hubs open by the end of the year, and the TEAMS B2B opportunity to unlock institutional healthcare needs remains ahead of us. Additionally, on impact, our efforts to serve those who serve others are constant.

This is a true brand differentiator and something our community deserves, and we are laser-focused on how we can scale this impact across our growing channels and geographies. Combining our momentum with these opportunities, we are optimistic about where we are going and how we can extend this unique growth story. Before diving deeper into product, brand, and market expansion, I want to update you on our supply chain following the Jordan developments we updated you on last quarter. While the WRO is still in effect, our own efforts are progressing. Our team and I recently traveled to Southeast Asia, both to support our existing Vietnamese supplier relationships and to further our diversification efforts.

We have secured additional capacity in Vietnam. Production is ramping, goods are being expedited, and inventory is improving real-time. In Q4, we are leveraging our existing partners to start our first production lines at facilities in Indonesia, which we expect will support a more meaningful mix of our business by the end of 2027. We are happy with our near-term progress, even as overall strength of demand extends some of our inventory replenishment efforts into Q1. Overall, this has been a complete team effort, particularly considering where our supplier base was positioned just 12 months ago. Now, let's turn to some of our product stories and where we are driving impact.

Our ongoing inflection in the business continues to showcase the growing sophistication in how we bring product and head-to-toe solutions to market. At the same time, our team is demonstrating the resilience needed to make impactful near-term changes to our calendar to mitigate supplier disruption. We delivered a range of impactful color collections throughout Q3, driving particular excitement on launch days. Success was broad-based and included our Spider-Man collaboration, back-to-school, a limited espresso drop, breast cancer awareness, and more. Our new durable FIBREx fabrication exceeded expectations, with highly positive social sentiment and customers specifically calling out our streetwear styling, our durable structured silhouette, and our overall design.

Speaking of design, we continue to differentiate our scrub pant offering with new waistband solutions across several of our best-selling styles. With our recent acquisition of V Coterie, we broadened our non-scrubwear opportunity with a dedicated collection of jewelry, pins, charms, and accessories designed specifically for healthcare professionals. This product officially debuted in August under what we are calling the CHARxMACY, creating a natural extension of the uniform by combining emotional engagement, personalization, and collectability. As we previously indicated, much of the original launch sold out, and we amassed a waiting list of nearly 10,000 healthcare professionals as we work to rebuild the assortment ahead of the holidays.

For Q4, we are off to a fast start with our fall drop, featuring a full return of our most requested color, espresso. The launch also showcased our unparalleled innovation flywheel, where we brought new fabric and style as part of our boucle outerwear assortment. Over the last few weeks, we have also started to get healthier from an inventory perspective, including in our core Rafaela and Kade styles, a process that will continue in the months ahead as we build supply and chase strong demand. We are excited to bring more newness across our FIBREx and FORMx fabrications, as well as our popular bag lines. The reach of our brand has never been broader or more impactful.

Impact trips are always a hallmark of our efforts to change healthcare. Through these trips, we travel the world with our FIGS ambassadors to extend support across a diverse range of healthcare needs. In July, we partnered with Operation Walk Los Angeles and St. Elizabeth Hospital to send a team to the Philippines to provide over 40 free joint replacements. We just recently continued our work in Kenya at the FIGS operating theater, the state-of-the-art hospital center we built in 2024. To date, this incredible facility has supported more than 2,500 surgeries that otherwise would not have been possible for this community of 500,000 Kenyans.

From advocacy to education, last month, we launched a dedicated FIGS moment with OOG Health, the AI-powered multidisciplinary education platform for healthcare professionals that is focused on redefining a broken continuing education industry and process. We made an investment in OOG two years ago, as we saw this opportunity align with our overall mission and significantly expand the ways that we can serve healthcare professionals. We have also now entered into a collaboration agreement with OOG that provides FIGS with a range of benefits, including on marketing, data, and AI.

On top of all these uniquely FIGS efforts, we have our big brand moments, and we are going big this week with the final chapter of our year-long Never Change campaign. This powerful bookend to our 2026 effort is designed to show all the good that lives within healthcare, and it is another example of what will never change across the community we are so proud to serve. Finally, I would be remiss not to mention our brand partner and friend, Noah Lyles, who People magazine just named as the Sexiest Man Alive for 2026.

To recognize our community, we partnered with People to show that sexy people save lives, putting a fun spotlight on six of our FIGS ambassadors as part of the overall feature. Turning to market expansion, starting with international. Third quarter net revenue growth surged 51% as our international mix approaches 20% of our total business. Our broad-based strength was particularly notable across Latin America, Mexico, the Middle East, and Europe, where we are seeing the benefits from our increased localization and community-building efforts. Overall, we are excited to see both strong traffic gains and incredible growth in our baseline days internationally, which we believe is another healthy sign of sustainable demand.

In TEAMS, we are showcasing our strategy to build a pipeline of larger opportunities. As examples, we are rolling out our recent partnerships with Bupa Dental Care, with nearly 400 dental centers in the U.K., and European Wax Center, with just over 1,000 aesthetic locations across the U.S. We believe these partnerships serve as blueprints for how to serve larger businesses in higher education, particularly as we continue to build solutions that simplify order complexity for a broader range of institutions. On the Community Hub side, we saw another quarter of double-digit comp store growth and non-comp performance exceeding our expectations.

All four of our 2026 openings are slated later this year: Tyson's Corner in the Washington, D.C. area, Fashion Square in Scottsdale, Aventura Mall in Miami, and Valley Fair near San Jose. We continue to progress with our real estate pipeline and expect a larger, more balanced store opening cadence in 2027. Importantly, the proof points remain compelling: a higher mix of new customers, a payback well under our two-year target, and accretive margins. In summary, our ongoing outperformance puts us on pace for our fastest full-year growth since our hyper-growth performance of 2021, when we were just about half the size we are today.

It has been a landmark year for FIGS, and I'm extremely proud of our ongoing execution and improving performance, even amid the noise of the macro, global uncertainty, and trade disruptions. As we focus on stepping into the new future, we are set up to grow Q4 in the mid-teens range on top of our blockbuster 33% growth in Q4 last year, accelerating our two-year stacked performance to nearly 50%. That is a powerful statement to exit the year. Along with top line, we are firmly focused on driving our adjusted EBITDA margin even higher. While we will not sacrifice investments to support the long-term aspirations of our brand, we do expect our discipline around profitability to continue in 2027.

Healthcare professionals are at the center of all we do. The needs they serve will not change, and neither will our commitment to showing up for them. With that, I will turn it over to Sarah to walk through our financial results and outlook.

Sarah Oughtred: Thanks, Trina. FIGS' strong Q3 continued an incredible four-quarter run of elevated growth, stronger profitability, and a bolstered balance sheet. I will start off with the details of our broad-based Q3 performance and then walk through our growing conviction in carrying this momentum into Q4 with our raised full-year outlook. Looking at the details of Q3, net revenues increased 26% year-over-year to $190.7 million, outpacing our outlook, calling for growth of approximately 20%. The breadth of our strength continued to stand out across scrubwear and non-scrubwear, domestically and abroad, and spanning our sales channels. Color launches were particularly impactful during the period, complemented by the ongoing success of our business-as-usual selling days without specific brand or product activations.

As anticipated, the back half of the quarter experienced some stockouts related to our Jordan supplier transition. Active customer growth accelerated for the fourth straight quarter, increasing 14% year-over-year to approach 3.2 million, and supported by a healthy balance of new and returning customers. Average order value increased 11% to $126, with balanced contributions from higher average unit retail and improved quality of sales. AUR gains were driven by both our early 2026 pricing action and product mix, while quality of sales was supported by lower discount and return rates. We were also encouraged by the continued trend in the reduction of days between purchases from our returning customers.

Together, these factors drove our trailing 12-month measure for net revenues per active customer up 13% to another FIGS record, $236. By category, scrubwear grew 24%, representing 83% of net revenues for the period. Core styles remained strong, powering demand throughout the quarter, while the breadth and impact of limited edition colors and expanded FIBREx styles drove robust sell-through and excitement. Non-scrubwear gained 35%, representing 17% of net revenues. The theme of broad-based growth was echoed here across categories. Underscrubs were supported by momentum in our core franchises and demand for new limited edition offerings. Outerwear was led by core scrub jackets and fleece, supporting our category optimism ahead of expansion efforts here in Q4.

Relative to historical cohorts, new customers are coming in with higher AOVs, above and beyond the impact of pricing, and sustaining this relative strength as they come back to the brand. Across returning customer cohorts, order frequency and spend are also improving. We believe these signs of increasing customer quality across our active customers are powerful building blocks for future growth. International growth continues to reflect the strong balance between new and returning customers. Trina highlighted the pronounced growth in Latin America, Mexico, the Middle East, and Europe, but we were also pleased with strong growth across the rest of the world.

While we have added 27 markets this year to reach 85 in total, we still generated nearly 90% of total international growth in Q3 from our existing comp markets. Gross margin for Q3 contracted 60 basis points to 69.3%, though ahead of our expectations and remaining quite strong. As a reminder, last year's comparable period was favorably impacted by our work to improve returns processing, which contributed to outsized performance for the period. While higher inventory reserves were also a headwind, we continued to see the positive impacts from pricing and efficiency efforts, which included costing, higher full price selling, and lower return rates.

Our selling expense for Q3 was $44.4 million, representing 23.3% of net revenues compared to 23.6% last year. The lower expense rate was primarily driven by net revenue leverage. Our successful ongoing work around outbound carrier optimization and fulfillment efficiencies has also helped offset ongoing fuel pressures, our supplier transitions, and international mix impact. Marketing expense for Q3 was $25.5 million, representing 13.4% of net revenues, down from 15.5% last year. Our Q3 performance reflected the impacts of net revenue leverage as well as digital marketing efficiencies. These gains continue to fuel great support in driving international brand awareness and other brand-enhancing partnerships. G&A for Q3 was $40 million, representing 21% of net revenues compared to 24.5% last year.

The lower G&A rate was primarily due to net revenue leverage, partially offset by investments in our team. Our Q3 operating margin jumped 520 basis points to 11.6% compared to 6.4% last year, while our adjusted EBITDA margin improved 410 basis points to 16.5% compared to 12.4% last year. As a reminder, our Q3 and year-to-date adjusted EBITDA margin only includes the portion of tariff refunds attributable to goods sold in those corresponding periods, excluding any benefits pertaining to fiscal 2025. Net income for the quarter totaled $19.3 million, or diluted EPS of $0.10, compared to net income of $8.7 million last year, or diluted EPS of $0.05.

On our balance sheet, we finished the quarter with record cash equivalents, and short-term investments of $331.2 million, approximately 10% higher than our prior peak in Q4 of 2025. This was a 37% increase year-over-year, even as we allocated approximately $60 million during the nine-month period for share repurchases and shifted to net share settlement of employee equity awards. Inventory decreased 20% year-over-year to $121.3 million, largely given the combined impact of our supplier transitions, strong sales performance, and ongoing optimization efforts. We expect inventory levels to remain down year-over-year for Q4 before beginning to rebuild in 2027.

Share repurchases during the quarter under our ongoing repurchase program totaled approximately $14 million at a weighted average price of $14.02 per share. Inclusive of the previously announced $100 million increase of our authorization, as of quarter end, we had approximately $105 million available for future repurchases under the program. Finally, capital expenditures for the quarter were $5.7 million, reflecting software capitalization, leasehold improvement, and Community Hub-related outlays for our Q4 openings. Now turning to our updated outlook. We have increased our Q4 expectations for net revenues and now expect approximately 15% year-over-year growth for the period. Combined with the underlying strength of our Q3 performance, we now expect full year 2026 net revenue growth of approximately 23.5% year-over-year.

This is ahead of our prior full year outlook, calling for approximately 20% growth and roughly double the high end of our original outlook provided on our February 2026 earnings call. We believe the growth and behavior of our active customer base are powerful indicators of demand, supporting the strong start to the fourth quarter, which included an incredibly successful fall drop in October and early improvements in our core in-stock levels. At the same time, we recognize that a significant share of the quarter remains ahead of us, where we are lapping last year's remarkable strength and execution.

Given the incredible demand we have seen year-to-date, we also believe the growth opportunity could have been higher if we were not experiencing limited edition stock-outs over the near term. Nonetheless, the implied two-year stack for Q4 of nearly 50% is a true testament to the momentum of our business. On the margin side, we now expect our full year GAAP gross margin to be approximately 69.9%, up from our prior outlook of approximately 69.5%.

As disclosed last quarter, this is inclusive of the $15.4 million reduction to cost of goods from the tariff refund recognized during Q2, an additional $1.9 million benefit in Q3 as impacted goods were sold during the period, and our sell-through expectations in Q4 for related inventory. While these refund-related expectations have not changed, the underlying gross margin expectations have improved with strong full price selling, more favorable product mix, and lower air freight than planned. For Q4, we expect a sizable year-over-year improvement, in part due to the large inventory write-off comparison from the year-ago period. Within SG&A, we would note two factors pertaining to Q4 expectations.

First, we expect a higher year-over-year selling expense rate driven by supplier transition costs and ongoing pressure on outbound freight. Second, we expect a lower year-over-year marketing expense rate driven by our ongoing efficiency efforts and the comparison to last year's ramp in Winter Olympics investments. Overall, we have increased our full-year operating margin outlook to approximately 12%, compared to our prior outlook of approximately 10.8%. We have also increased our full-year adjusted EBITDA margin outlook to approximately 15.8%, up from our prior range of between 14.8% and 15%. We are set up for a great finish to a great year. Our full-year net revenue growth is planned to be the highest since 2021.

Our adjusted EBITDA margins are planned to inflect 650 basis points from trough levels in 2024, and our balance sheet has never been stronger. Just as important, we are positioned to sustain both top and bottom line momentum into 2027. We are now happy to take your questions. Operator?

We debuted our new Team Store in March, and we have steadily iterated, and brought more and more functionality throughout this year. We are also working with bigger institutions that they use some of the self-serve, but they also want some handholding and concierge support, and we have that as well.

Managing these large institutions that some of the largest we have ever had in our history, they have multiple locations, they have different departments, and there is just more complexity. I think we are learning a lot, and we are really looking to serve them in more and more ways. It is an exciting time. We talked about Bupa, we talked about European Wax Center. These are very large institutions that are turning to FIGS to help them standardize and professionalize and brand their institution. That is a great sign of how we are going to be able to partner with concierge clinics, with private practice, and more types of healthcare institutions. Healthcare is everywhere.

Longevity, wellness, aesthetics, it is becoming just part of our lives in more and more ways, and becoming more and more consumerized. We are at the center of supporting this evolution of what healthcare will be today, tomorrow, and long into the future. We have a lot more impactful partnerships that we are going to be sharing with you soon. We cannot share them yet, but really, really exciting.

Rick Patel: Great. I also had a question on non-scrubs. How much of your customer base that buys scrubs today is currently buying non-scrubs? We have the revenue figures, but curious about the attachment rate and whether this is a concentrated business, and to what extent engaging more existing customers can be an opportunity going forward.

Sarah Oughtred: Our attachment rate differs each quarter a little bit with seasonality, but we are in that mid-20s on attachment rate, and we are seeing that penetration increase year-over-year, given our efforts to expand and offer some really exciting things within non-scrubwear. We do know that as each customer makes more purchases, more cumulative purchases with FIGS, that is where they start to add on more for non-scrubwear. We really like the dynamics that we are seeing. We think there is still quite a bit of runway there, as we continue to expand our outerwear assortment, add new fabrications within our under-scrubs, continue to have the color work that has been successful with our footwear.

We've, as you know, recently added on this year, the compression socks and ThermaC is the next exciting thing that will really add some of those elements that, with your uniform, you want these non-scrubwear components to really help personalize your look. We do see that as an opportunity, and think we'll be really excited about all the things that will continue to add to that attachment rate as we go forward.

Operator: Your next question comes from the line of Nathan Feather with Morgan Stanley. Your line is open. Please go ahead.

Nathan Feather: Hey, everyone. Really exciting to see the traction across the business. I just wanted to follow up on that TEAMS question. As you've started to get into some larger partners here, what have been the key gating factors? You're going to the meetings for people that aren't converting today. What are the things you still need to build or add to better serve those? Now that you've had a little bit more data, how should we think about kind of the sales timeline and cycle for that business, especially for kind of the larger versus smaller clients?

Trina Spear: Thanks, Nathan. I mean, I think we are on our way. There's nothing that's stopping us from continue to scale that business and continue to bring on very, very large healthcare institutions. What I'll say is that this year, we're excited to see that the TEAMS growth is inflecting higher and it's outpacing total revenues. It's a huge opportunity as the healthcare world, like I said, is becoming more consumerized to engage in super meaningful ways. I do think that the foundation we've built from a technology standpoint has enabled us to scale much faster and really view institutions into two buckets. Really, are you looking to self-serve?

Are you looking to have each individual or each employee within your institution order on their own because you have a variety of locations, or you want everyone to get their own uniform? That's been a huge unlock from the TEAMS perspective. From a concierge larger institution perspective, we've really built a lot of technology that's enabled us to serve them as well and have stipend functionality, for instance. So if you want to have an institution spend $400 per employee a year and you want to be able to have that in their account, we now have that functionality as an example. I really think that there's much, much more to come here.

Like I said, a lot in the works, a lot in the pipeline. To your point, the sales cycle is longer. I think with the process we've created, with the team that we've brought on, we have an incredible sales team, we've been able to take that sales cycle down to a number of months from potentially even in some cases a year or so. So, a really exciting time to utilize technology, utilize capabilities, utilize a team that's passionate and excited about this community to drive incredible relationships that will pay dividends over the long run, given the retention rate that these institutions have with us, much higher than e-comm.

It's a really great business, and we're just getting started.

Nathan Feather: Very exciting. Thank you.

Operator: Your last question comes from the line of Ashley Owens with KeyBanc Capital Markets. Your line is open. Please go ahead.

Ashley Owens: Hi. Great. Thanks for taking our questions. Maybe first, just to circle back on international and that comment on the 90% growth in existing markets. I know you've talked in the past about some of those markets reaching a tipping point where greater awareness and word of mouth can bring down that acquisition cost pretty meaningfully. I would just be curious if you're starting to see more of your markets reach that point, and is that beginning to show up in the profitability of those markets as well?

Trina Spear: I mean, I think that our strategy around our international markets around going deep versus going broad has really enabled us to invest appropriately given the size and scale of each market, right? We are profitable in every market, right? Why is that? It is because we have really been strategic in terms of how we are investing across the funnel, how we are localizing market by market in terms of our brand activations, in terms of our e-commerce experience, in terms of what channels we have. We really see incredible momentum across especially the markets like Mexico, Latin America, the Middle East, and Europe.

Like I said, really excited, earlier days in APAC, but really excited about what that is going to do in the future. We are focused on not just growing for growth's sake. You know this. We are really looking to do it in a disciplined way and focused on CAC and focused on what those investments will return over time. We really feel good about where we sit today and how we are going to continue to drive engagement, awareness. I mean, we still have such low brand awareness in all of these countries.

We really need to have people know who FIGS is because if you are a healthcare professional, there are 140 million healthcare professionals around the world, and we have 3 million, 3.2. That is about 137 million healthcare professionals that do not yet know about us. Much, much more to do.

Ashley Owens: Okay. Understood. Maybe just last for me, looking ahead here to 2027 on some of the product costs, I'd just be curious as you're starting to place buys for next year, what you're seeing or hearing from the vendor base in terms of the underlying product costs. Then when you put that together with some things like tariffs and then obviously freight, is the sourcing environment becoming more or less favorable, do you think, heading into next year? Thanks.

Sarah Oughtred: Sure. Many puts and takes around margin and how we think about how 2027 will play out. I mean, if we start with the largest one being the tariff refund. We did receive $15.4 million that was a benefit to gross margin in Q2, and then the remainder of that refund was a reduction to inventory. As that inventory is sold in Q3 and Q4, we're seeing additional benefit there. We did see an additional $1.9 million in benefit in Q3 and expect to see more in Q4. The benefit to gross margin for all of 2026 is about 250 basis points of benefit.

That will be a headwind on gross margin from a year-over-year perspective, just given that those tariff refunds were one-time in nature. The other component impacting us is also our transition from Jordan into Vietnam. We have a slightly different duty structure, and we are seeing some slightly higher costs just at the onset with some of our fabrications that we can continue to work on over time. Freight has impacted our P&L, both in terms of inbound freight, which would hit our gross margin, as well as outbound freight for our packages going out. For now, we are continuing to monitor.

We're not necessarily planning for any reductions at this point, but if we do see a notable reduction, that could be a benefit into next year. I would say that as we think about gross margin, I think the normalized rate this year would be in that 67.5-ish, and I think we'll kind of be in that range for next year, given some of the puts and takes.

Ashley Owens: Awesome. Appreciate the color. Thanks, and best of luck.

Operator: We have reached the end of the Q&A session. I will now turn the call back to Trina Spear, CEO, for closing remarks.

Trina Spear: Thank you, everybody. Just want to say one final thing. Don't forget to buy FIGS for all your doctors, your nurses, your veterinarians, your dentists, all the healthcare workers in your life. Hope you have a wonderful holiday season. We'll see you next call.

Operator: This concludes today's call. Thank you for attending. You may now disconnect.

Kalshi hit with injunction over sports contracts on California tribal lands

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

A federal judge has granted a preliminary injunction barring Kalshi from offering sports-event contracts on the lands of Blue Lake Rancheria and the Chicken Ranch Rancheria of Me-Wuk Indians.

A federal judge has granted a preliminary injunction barring Kalshi from offering sports-event contracts on the lands of two California tribes.

In a Wednesday order, U.S. District Judge Jacqueline Scott Corley ruled that the injunction covers contracts tied to sports including football, basketball, baseball, soccer, tennis, and Ultimate Fighting Championship events.

The tribes — Blue Lake Rancheria and the Chicken Ranch Rancheria of Me-Wuk Indians — must provide boundary maps by Nov. 13, the judge said.

Kalshi is required to report the status of the steps it has taken to comply with the injunction within 21 days, according to the order.

Sports and gaming attorney Daniel Wallach described the order as the first preliminary injunction secured by tribes against Kalshi nationwide, calling it "a major development in tribal gaming litigation."

The tribes filed the lawsuit in July 2025, alleging that Kalshi's sports event contracts constitute unauthorized Class III gaming under the Indian Gaming Regulatory Act (IGRA) and violate the tribes' gaming ordinances.

Kalshi has argued that its sports event contracts are federally regulated swaps under the Commodity Exchange Act (CEA), and that the CEA's grant of exclusive jurisdiction to the Commodity Futures Trading Commission preempts tribal gaming law.

The judge initially denied the tribes' motion for a preliminary injunction in November 2025. The tribes then appealed, and a Ninth Circuit panel partially reversed the decision in September.

"Reversing the district court's denial of a preliminary injunction in part, the panel held that the tribes were likely to succeed in their claims that Kalshi's sports event contracts on tribal lands violated IGRA and the tribes' gaming ordinances," the panel wrote.

The Ninth Circuit panel also held that the CEA's "exclusive" jurisdiction did not extend to Kalshi's sports event contracts.

The Block has reached out to Kalshi for comment.

Kalshi faces mounting legal challenges, with more states taking action against the prediction markets platform.

On Wednesday, Arkansas Attorney General Tim Griffin sued Kalshi for allegedly operating an illegal gambling platform in the state.

"It doesn't take a genius to understand that wagering money on whether the Razorbacks win or lose is gambling," Griffin said, adding that such practice is "squarely prohibited" by Arkansas law.

Last week, Utah also filed criminal charges against Kalshi and two company executives, accusing them of illegally promoting online gambling. Utah has also sued Kalshi and two Susquehanna trading firms for allegedly operating an illegal gambling business.

Equinix Opens SG6 in Singapore, Expanding AI-Ready Capacity in One of Asia-Pacific's Most Connected Markets

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Latest data center combines liquid cooling, renewable energy and global connectivity to support next-generation computing demands

REDWOOD CITY, Calif., Nov. 6, 2026 /PRNewswire/ -- Equinix, Inc. (Nasdaq: EQIX), the world's digital infrastructure companyÂ®, today announced the opening of SG6, its sixth International Business Exchangeâ¢ (IBXÂ®) data center in Singapore. Designed to support AI, high-performance computing and data-intensive workloads, SG6 expands Equinix's global footprint of more than 280 data centers and provides customers with the infrastructure needed to connect, innovate and scale in an increasingly AI-driven economy.

As businesses worldwide accelerate investments in AI, cloud and digital services, demand is growing for highly connected infrastructure that can support next-generation applications while advancing sustainability objectives. Located in one of Asia-Pacific's most strategically connected markets, SG6 combines private interconnection, global ecosystems and advanced infrastructure to help organizations deploy distributed digital workloads across regions.

"Singapore sits at the intersection of global commerce, digital technology and sustainable development," said Adaire Fox-Martin, Chief Executive Officer and President, Equinix. "Equinix has been investing in Singapore for nearly 25 years, and our new facility will further expand our presence as the neutral exchange where enterprises connect to the clouds, networks and partners they need to innovate and grow. Singapore is one of our most important markets globally, and our continued investment here is a testament to the central role it plays in the digital economy."

SG6 is among the first data centers developed under Singapore's Data Centre Call for Application framework and is designed to support high-density AI deployments through advanced cooling technologies, including proprietary cooling systems and liquid-cooling capabilities that accommodate power-intensive GPU infrastructure.

The opening of SG6 further advances Equinix's Future First sustainability strategy and aligns with the company's science-based commitment to achieve net zero emissions by 2040 and 100% renewable energy coverage globally by 2030 across its retail IBXs data centers. Equinix's Singapore data centers have maintained 100% renewable energy coverage since 2020. SG6 is designed to achieve a Power Usage Effectiveness (PUE) of 1.20 and is targeting Green Mark Platinum certification. The facility also incorporates rooftop and facade solar photovoltaic systems, rainwater harvesting capabilities and lower-embodied-carbon construction materials to support more efficient operations and responsible growth.

"Asia-Pacific is at the forefront of digital transformation, with AI adoption, cloud expansion and data-intensive workloads driving unprecedented demand for high-performance digital infrastructure," said Cyrus Adaggra, President, Asia-Pacific, Equinix. "Singapore remains a strategic gateway for businesses serving the region, connecting key markets, talent and innovation ecosystems. SG6 strengthens our ability to support customers as they scale across Asia-Pacific, providing the resilient, interconnected and sustainable infrastructure they need to accelerate innovation and capture new growth opportunities."

With an initial investment of approximately $290 million, SG6 provides 8 MW of capacity in its first phase, delivering approximately 1,550 cabinets. Upon full build-out, the facility will offer up to 20 MW of IT capacity.

Equinix also plans to open a Co-Innovation Facility (CIF) at SG6 in partnership with the National University of Singapore in 2027. The facility will focus on advancing technologies that improve data center sustainability and energy efficiency, including liquid cooling, cognitive digital twins and battery energy storage systems.

Since entering Singapore in 2002, Equinix has expanded from a single data center to six facilities, creating one of Asia-Pacific's most densely interconnected digital infrastructure hubs. Equinix's Singapore operations connect enterprises to a rich ecosystem of cloud, IT, financial services, communications, media and technology providers, while supporting connectivity to global markets through more than 17 subsea cable systems.

About EquinixEquinix, Inc. (Nasdaq: EQIX) shortens the path to boundless connectivity anywhere in the world. Its digital infrastructure, data center footprint and interconnected ecosystems empower innovations that enhance our work, life and planet. Equinix connects economies, countries, organizations and communities, delivering seamless digital experiences and cutting-edge AIâquickly, efficiently and everywhere.

‘Kamen Rider’ Studio Toei Frames Generative AI Rules, Sets First Video Games for 2027

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Toei, the Japanese studio giant behind “Kamen Rider” and the Super Sentai series that became “Power Rangers,” has drawn up rules for generative AI and started using AI in its film and video production, president and CEO Yoshimura Fumio said at the Taiwan Creative Content Fest (TCCF) in Taipei.

A dedicated team at the company is testing how far AI can be used in actual content production, and Toei is building an AI system that helps less experienced producers estimate budgets. Related Stories Robert Pattinson to Receive 40th American Cinematheque Award A Version of Zendaya's Viral 'Spider-Man' Tee Is Now Available to Shop Online

“We have put in place rules for the use of generative AI, and from this year we have started using AI in our film and video production,” Yoshimura said. “We are already working on things like creating backgrounds with AI.”

Toei’s back-office departments also use AI for routine tasks, data processing and internal queries, and the company is looking at applying it to the creative supervision of IP merchandise. Yoshimura pointed to “Dreams of Violets,” a live-action feature made entirely with generative AI that was selected for the Tribeca Festival in June. The 75-minute film was reportedly made by two creators for around $2,000.

“I believe AI is not something that takes away jobs, but a tool that expands the possibilities of expression and business,” he said.

Toei also put a timeline on its move into games. Toei Games, the label launched this year to mark the company’s 75th anniversary, will release “Hino” in early 2027, with “Killa” and “Debug Nephemee” following from spring 2027. All three are original titles from outside creators rather than adaptations of Toei IP, and will launch on Steam before rolling out to Nintendo Switch, PlayStation and Xbox.

“We aim to bring game creators’ talent on board over the long term as a new vein of IP creation, and make games the starting point for stories in place of novels and comics,” Yoshimura said.

The keynote also mapped out how Toei is taking “Kamen Rider,” which turned 55 this year, beyond Japanese TV. Yoshimura held up the franchise as his main example of what the company calls the “evergreening” of IP. The first series premiered in 1971, and the Showa-era shows that ran until 1988 built its core fanbase. The Heisei-era series that began in 2000 handed the franchise down from parents to children, while events, merchandise, manga, anime and stage shows have since carried it to a third generation.

The current series, “Kamen Rider Zeztz,” was rolled out almost simultaneously around the world, including Taiwan, the rest of Asia and North America, which Yoshimura called a big step for the group. Toei is also pushing into India. “Kamen Rider” was introduced there at a Japanese IP event in 2025, and the strong response led to a deal with a local Disney-affiliated company.

“Considering the population size and rising incomes from a growing middle class, I think India can be called an extremely promising market in the medium to long term,” Yoshimura said. “We expect it will take time before this takes root as a business, but it’s important to build connections now and steadily grow our partnerships and fan base.”

In April, Toei launched The Kamen Rider Animated, an animation label. Its first project teams the studio with Aniplex, the company behind “Demon Slayer,” and Shirogumi, which worked on Yamazaki Takashi’s “Godzilla Minus One,” on a new “Kamen Rider” property with a scale and style of action only animation can deliver. A live-action label, The Kamen Rider Premium, will make full-scale hero action films for a wider audience, including international viewers and people who don’t follow the TV shows.

Under its Toei New Wave 2033 medium- to long-term plan, the company aims to raise the share of its sales that comes from overseas from 30% to 50%, with Asia the current focus. Yoshimura cited estimates that the global entertainment and media market is growing by an average of 3.4% a year. He added that streaming now lets K-pop, Japanese anime, Indian films and Chinese microdramas travel the world without being changed.

“Now that content rooted in local cultures and histories, like ‘Shogun,’ is crossing racial and national borders and captivating people around the world, I’d say the main battlefield for our business is overseas,” he said.

During the audience Q&A, Yoshimura said that before “Power Rangers,” Toei exported its Japanese series unchanged, and they struggled to win over Western audiences. The breakthrough came from keeping only the characters and rebuilding the cast and drama in the U.S.

“I believe this approach of localization is indispensable for spreading characters around the world,” he said. “We also understand that, unlike animation, live-action work in particular is not something that is readily accepted as-is in each country.”

“Power Rangers” has now run in the U.S. for more than 30 years. In 2025, the original “Mighty Morphin Power Rangers” was relaunched as “Mighty Morphin Power Rangers: Re-Ignition,” with newly designed toys from Hasbro, a “Fortnite” collaboration and a Lego line in the works. Toei used a similar remake model in the Philippines. “Voltes V: Legacy,” the 2023 live-action version of Toei’s 1977 anime “Super Electromagnetic Machine Voltes V,” peaked at a 58% rating there and was released in Japanese cinemas the following year.

The company is also building stories from scratch in other markets. In February, it signed a strategic co-production partnership with Thai producer-distributor M Studio. Together with M Studio and Korea’s Showbox, it is adapting Ito Junji’s “Long Hair in the Attic” as a premium Thai film for Asian audiences. The film will open in Thailand first before heading to Japan and other territories.

In co-production, Toei backed “Dear Stranger,” a Japan-Taiwan-U.S. film from director Mariko Tetsuya that stars Gwei Lun-mei and Nishijima Hidetoshi. It was shot entirely in New York and released last September. The project was selected for the Hong Kong Asia Film Financing Forum’s HAF and raised money abroad from the start, with Taiwanese investors including TAICCA’s TICP 2.0 program. Toei’s Kyoto studio is also supporting the Japan shoot of “Iris,” an action thriller from a U.S. independent producer.

Toei is the only company in Japan that owns and runs two studio lots, one in Tokyo and one in Kyoto. The Tokyo lot houses Japan’s only permanent 270-degree LED volume stage.

Toei is also making short-form content. “Everyday Host Club” is a short anime of 24 episodes that run about three minutes each. “Meshimori Onna,” produced at the Kyoto studio for a client, is a vertical period drama of 30 episodes that run one to two minutes. Yoshimura suggested that vertical formats could one day become the norm even in cinemas.

On Taiwan, he noted that in 2025 Japanese films were the most-released titles in the market and second only to U.S. films at the box office. Toei titles released there include “Midnight Diner,” “Shin Kamen Rider,” “Howling Village” and “Oshi no Ko.” Local broadcaster YoYo TV airs “Kamen Rider,” and Toei is expanding live character shows in Taiwan with a local partner. The studio also invested in the Taiwanese horror film “The Rope Curse 4.” Yoshimura said he wants long-term tokusatsu partnerships and international co-productions with Taiwan.

“We are looking for partners with whom we can plan, make and deliver compelling stories to the world together,” he said.

Yoshimura joined Toei in 1988. He worked in live events, licensing and digital distribution, including some of Japan’s early mobile content and subscription streaming services, and became president in 2023.

FA and WSL Football launch major study into ACL injuries in women’s football

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

To help deliver timely coverage of women's football, we may use AI tools to support content creation and editing. While we strive for accuracy and editorial quality, readers should independently verify important information.

The Football Association and WSL Football have teamed up with Imperial College London on a new study into ACL injuries, the knee problem that has cost the women’s game some of its biggest names in recent seasons. All clubs across the Barclays WSL and WSL2 are being asked to take part, from the players themselves to the medical, performance and coaching staff around them.

Announced on Thursday, the project is being run as a PhD programme funded by the FA. Imperial researcher Hanna Lehtovirta, based in the university’s Department of Mechanical Engineering, is leading the work, with Fortius Clinic knee surgeon Simon Ball also involved. According to the FA’s announcement, the aim is to find out why anterior cruciate ligament ruptures keep happening in the elite women’s game and to turn those findings into practical prevention advice.

The research is split into two stages. In the first, players complete surveys covering their injury record, how much they train and play, and hormonal, nutritional and psychological factors. Anyone who has ruptured an ACL since the 2018-19 campaign will have their answers set against those of players who have avoided the injury, in the hope of spotting patterns linked to risk.

The second stage goes back to the same group for interviews, and this time staff are brought in too. The FA says plenty of earlier research has looked at single factors, often in a lab, and that far less is known about how they combine in the daily routine of a professional squad. Lehtovirta said her team would be looking at everything “from minutes played and injury prevention practices at clubs to how players developed before they entered professional football.”

ESPN reports that data gathering began in June and that the study is set to run for three years.

The numbers explain the urgency. Research cited by ESPN suggests women are around twice as likely as men to suffer an ACL injury, and roughly two in every three cases happen with no contact from another player. England captain Leah Williamson and Beth Mead both missed the 2023 World Cup after rupturing the ligament, and Vivianne Miedema has also had to come back from the injury during her time in England.

Ritan Mehta, the FA’s Head of Women’s Medical, was clear about the scale of the problem. “ACL injuries remain one of the biggest challenges facing female footballers,” she said. “While significant progress has been made in understanding the factors that may contribute to these injuries, there is still much more to learn.”

WSL Football’s Medical Governance Lead, Dr Abosede Ajayi, pointed to the mix of football, healthcare and academia behind the project, saying the findings could “inform medical standards across the women’s game”. Ball put it more bluntly: “ACL injury is devastating for professional athletes,” he said, adding that “prevention is better than cure!”

For more on how ACL injuries have hit the women’s game, read our reports Spurs confirm record signing Alice Sombath has ruptured her ACL and Project ACL puts WSL injury crisis back in focus with cross-league research.

The English study follows a similar move in the United States, where the NWSL and its players’ association joined Project ACL in April. The FA also marked the launch on social media:

Together with the @BarclaysWSL, we have launched a new research project to better understand ACL injuries in women’s football.

Bitcoin, Ether ETFs post $530M outflows as election gains evaporate

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

US spot Bitcoin and Ether ETFs shed a combined $530 million Thursday as Bitcoin fell below $76,000 and US stocks tumbled.

US spot Bitcoin and Ether exchange-traded funds (ETFs) posted about $530 million in net outflows Thursday as cryptocurrency markets fell sharply.

Bitcoin (BTC) ETFs recorded $430 million in net outflows while Ether (ETH) funds lost around $100 million, according to SoSoValue data.

The reversal followed $128 million in combined inflows Wednesday, when Bitcoin briefly climbed above $80,000 after Democrats’ victories in the US midterm elections.

Bitcoin fell back below $76,000 and the tech-heavy Nasdaq Composite plunged 5.5% on Thursday, which was its biggest single-day decline since April 2025, according to Reuters.

Fidelity’s Wise Origin Bitcoin Fund (FBTC) led Thursday’s withdrawals with roughly $200 million in outflows after attracting around $237 million on Wednesday, according to Farside Investors data.

BlackRock’s iShares Bitcoin Trust (IBIT) recorded another $105 million in net outflows. The latest withdrawals extended IBIT’s outflow streak to five consecutive sessions, with more than $1 billion withdrawn since Oct. 30.

Daily spot Bitcoin ETF flows by issuer since Oct. 30. Source: Farside Investors

Bitcoin ETFs’ year-to-date net outflows stood at around $510 million following Thursday’s closing bell.

Crypto analyst Michaël van de Poppe said on X Thursday that Bitcoin could find a near-term bottom Friday, potentially after dipping below $75,000, pointing to the severity of the Nasdaq’s decline.

“I expect Nasdaq to bottom tomorrow. The same for Bitcoin, potentially after sweeping $75,000,” he wrote.

US stock market heat map. Source: Yahoo Finance

Dean Lyulkin, CEO of Cardiff and founder of investment publication The Dean’s List, said the current market environment differs from the period leading up to the 2008 financial crisis.

Related: Bitcoin crashes 6% to 7-week low as US stocks erase election gains

“The global financial crisis wasn’t caused by Democrats taking Congress,” Lyulkin said in comments shared with Cointelegraph, noting that the Federal Reserve had already raised interest rates substantially and the housing market was weakening before the crisis.

While acknowledging vulnerabilities in commercial real estate, private credit and potentially excessive artificial intelligence spending, Lyulkin argued that household balance sheets and the banking system are considerably stronger today.

Is the Gyokeres iron hot enough for Hull?

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

I’m here for our book launch which takes place tonight, but also for our game against Hull tomorrow. Mikel Arteta will meet the press this afternoon ahead of that one, and we’ll bring you all the stories on Arseblog News. I don’t think there’s gonna be anything mind-blowing, it looked like we came through the Slavia Prague game without any issues, so fingers crossed that’s the case.

I suppose I’m mildly curious as to whether Viktor Gyokeres’ performance on Wednesday will see him keep his place up front. He got a great goal, provided an assist for Noni Madueke, and probably should have had another assist when he headed back across goal for Mikel Merino, only for the Spaniard to head over the bar when we know he can do better from those situations.

He’s a player who seems to need confidence and rhythm, and on that basis it would make some sense to keep him in the starting line-up. After the last Interlull, when he scored in four successive games for Sweden, I made the case that giving him a start in our first game back would make some sense to try and harness some of what was a good goal scoring run. Strike while the iron is hot and all that.

I looked at a home game against Leeds as the kind of fixture that would suit him. He scored two against them at home last season, and one at Elland Road, but when the time came Mikel Arteta picked Kai Havertz up front. Speaking about the decision to start Gyokeres on Wednesday in Prague, the manager said:

It was a game we believed would be really good for him, for the demands the opponent puts in and the spaces he could use. He’s done it really well, not just for the assist and the goal, but he was really connected to the team. It’s really great news for him, because he really needed that performance.

The question will be does Arteta think Hull at home will be as good for him as Slavia Prague away? After a bright start to the season they haven’t won any of their last seven but they have drawn five of those. Chances are they’re going to come and sit deep, using the blueprint we’ve seen many teams deploy against Arsenal in the past. If a Gyokeres game, if we can call it that, requires the opposition to leave spaces he can use (as per Arteta’s quote above), then maybe this isn’t one of those.

However, on paper, and with all due respect to tomorrow’s opposition, if Hull at home isn’t a Gyokeres game, then it’s hard not to be a bit worried. Personally, I’d be all right to see him start this one, and if it doesn’t work then you have other options. Havertz is the obvious one, but Mikel Merino is an alternative as well with his ability to find spaces inside the opponent’s penalty area. Ideally though, it’s a bit of a building block of a game for the Swedish international. We need him to deliver goals and assists, the last few weeks have been a bit more positive in that regard, and ultimately it’s important that the manager recognises the moments in a season when he might have to choose one player over another for the overall benefit of the team.

Which is to say, I look forward very much to seeing Declan Rice starting up front against Hull tomorrow, and Arteta revealing that his tech-boffins have invented a gizmo which will allow Rice to player 180 minutes in every game. Plus injury time. I kid, obviously, but I suppose we should know by now that regardless of what we think about team selections, use of players, and everything else, this manager will never be even remotely swayed by external noise – and that’s a good thing most of the time!

We will talk a bit more about the Hull game in a quick preview podcast on Patreon a little later on. Because of the book launch, we’ll be recording before the manager meets the press, so bear that in mind.

Hopefully catch lots of you later on at Big Red – and here’s a nice piece on the photos in the book. Just a reminder again that those who have registered to come to the launch, you can purchase the book via the email sent to you (check your junk folders if you don’t see it), so you can simply come and collect on the night. We can also facilitate sales via credit card etc if you don’t want to do that.

If you purchased via the Patreon pre-sale, those books are not for collection tomorrow, and will be shipped to you in due course. There is also some scope to come along on spec on the night, and we should have copies available but obviously priority goes to those who registered. We will then have a final batch of books going on general sale online on Saturday evening after the Hull game. Full details of that to follow here on site and via Arseblog on BlueSky.

Best way to grind Research Points in Godzilla: Destroy All Monsters Melee Remastered

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Godzilla: Destroy All Monsters Melee Remastered has a fair number of things to unlock, and you’ll need plenty of Research Points. You get Research Points by just playing the game, but there’s an easy way to fast track the process.

Let’s find out the best way to grind Research Points in the remastered version.

The method I have mentioned there was present in the original version, but the grind was significantly easier. Remastered version has made things harder, but the core method remains the same.

Your first task is to unlock Kiryu from its research tree and pick the Survival mode. Then, pick Monster Island as the location and map to kickstart the process.

Shoot a rocket as soon as the match begins, and the AI will always block it. Then, get close to your enemy and grab them with the window that you have created. Once you grab the enemy, get close to the wall just behind the spot where the AI spawns.

You’ll find a lava pit at the location, and you can then use Kiryu’s fierce attack to launch the enemy over the wall without breaking it. Now, the enemy will take 10 damage per second due to the lava.

Once your main tasks is done, your next job is to keep them locked in the pit without failure. If they try to escape, grab and toss them in the lava once again.

When the enemy is in the pit, the wall will usually remain intact. However, the enemy can sometimes wall the hug and take no damage. If that happens, don’t break the wall yourself. Instead, bait them to do that task instead for you. Your only focus is to ensure that the enemy stays in the lava and keeps taking damage.

Airbnb is building an AI travel agent

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Airbnb is planning to launch an AI travel agent next year. The company shared details of the project during its Q3 2026 earnings call on Thursday, reiterating its intention to "build an AI-native Airbnb."

"AI is the best thing to ever happen to Airbnb. It's changing both how we develop products and how we operate," Airbnb said in a letter to shareholders. "AI is helping our teams move faster, and in Q3 alone, we shipped more than 50 products and features — nearly 70 percent more than a year ago."

Now Airbnb intends to add an AI travel agent to that collection. While still under development, the company says its new AI agent is being designed to help users research, plan, and book trips on its platform.

"We’re developing a custom-designed, AI-native experience that builds the interface around your question," said Airbnb. "Rather than a chat window, our agent draws on a library of hundreds of travel-specific interface elements — maps, photos, comparisons, calendars, itineraries — and composes them in real time to match what you’re looking for."

CEO Brian Chesky elaborated during Airbnb's earnings call, stating that the AI agent will "feel a little bit like generative AI" due to its ability to respond to users' requests with relevant custom interfaces for specific travel needs. Chesky further stated that the agent will have a "post-chat-based interface," being less text-focused, though it will still retain a "chat element."

"If you want a car rental, the agent generates this little UI of a car, and it's [presented] beautifully in a custom-generated interface," said Chesky. "If you want to invite co-travellers, the agent helps you."

Airbnb's AI agent will also help resolve issues once a booking has already been made, including changing reservations or assisting with refunds and rebookings. Interestingly, Airbnb states that the AI will provide 24-hour safety support as well, though did not elaborate on exactly what type of assistance it will offer.

"Our agent will help guests explore options, make decisions with the people they’re travelling with, and take action directly in Airbnb," Airbnb wrote. "Over time, we want our agent to help guests plan their entire trip in one place, across homes, hotels, services, experiences, and more."

This won't be restricted to bookings that are available on Airbnb either. Chesky stated that the AI agent will be able to go "outside the walls of Airbnb," though noted it will stay within the realm of travel.

"Today's AI agents, like [Meta's AI agent] Muse and [invite-only AI assistant] Instinct, are good at simple routine tasks like making a restaurant reservation or scheduling an appointment," Chesky said. "But planning a trip is different. Travel is aspirational. Discovering where to go and where to stay is part of the experience… I don't think the general-purpose agents are the best to do that. That is why we are building our own AI agent."

Airbnb's new AI travel agent is expected to be released in the first half of 2027.

This upcoming AI travel agent isn't to be confused with Airbnb's AI customer support assistant, which is already in operation. Airbnb states that its AI assistant resolved around 40 percent of all customer support issues without any human agents in Q3, an increase from approximately 30 percent last quarter. In Q2, Airbnb's AI agent resolved nearly 45 percent of issues that specifically began in the tool.

Airbnb reports that its revenue grew 19 percent to $4.9 billion in Q3, bringing in a net income of $1.9 billion.

Germany news: Trial of alleged 'Reichsbürger' group ringleaders begins

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Four members of the banned far-right "Kingdom of Germany" are on trial for belonging to a criminal organization. Meanwhile, a new study warns data centers could lead to water stress in Germany. Follow DW for more.

Read below for the top headlines from across Germany on Friday, November 6:

Four alleged ringleaders of the banned far-right "Kingdom of Germany" (KRD) are expected to appear before the Düsseldorf Higher Regional Court this morning.

The group is part of a conspiracy theorist movement that rejects the legitimacy of the modern German republic.

Authorities banned the KRD in May 2025 after monitoring its activities for more than a decade. Since then, the four defendants, including the group's so-called "King" Peter Fitzek, have been held in pre-trial detention.

They have been charged with membership in a criminal organization and other offenses, including document forgery and tax evasion amounting to millions of euros.

The Federal Prosecutor's Office accuses Fitzek and his co-defendants of seeking to replace the German republic with their own system. They are alleged to have set up "pseudo-state structures" such as a banking and insurance system, as well as a "registration office" that issued fictitious identity documents.

At the time the group was banned, hundreds of police officers carried out raids on 15 properties across Germany. They seized "further evidence of the association's anti-constitutional goals and activities," according to the Interior Ministry.

Authorities said the group had acquired land through donations with the aim of reducing the territory of Germany.

According to the prosecutor's office, they sought to expand their purported territory to the borders of the German Empire of 1871.

The group is estimated to have had around 1,000 followers at the time it was banned.

To view this video please enable JavaScript, and consider upgrading to a web browser that supports HTML5 video

Guten morgen from the DW newsroom here on the banks of the Rhine in Bonn.

About 70 kilometers (43 miles) further north, the trial of four alleged ringleaders of the banned "Kingdom of Germany" (KRD) is expected to begin Friday in the city of Düsseldorf.

KRD is part of Germany's "Reichsbürger" movement, which rejects the modern German state and its structure as illegitimate. The group is accused of setting up its own structures, including a banking and insurance system.

Also making news, the German parliament is voting on whether to impose harsher penalties for the use of date rape drugs.

And, a new study suggests the expansion of data centers in Germany catering to the AI boom could lead to water stress.
